US waiver eases risks for India, China, but won't alter Russian crude flows
Waiver to reduce uncertainty over Russian crude: CERA
India, China to maintain Russian crude purchases
Refiners to seek alternatives amid policy risks
The US waiver on Russian crude has reduced uncertainty for Indian and Chinese refiners, giving them greater confidence to continue purchasing economically attractive Russian grades while keeping alternative supply options open in case the situation changes, analysts and industry sources said Oct. 22.
The waiver is expected to preserve, rather than materially alter, Russian crude flows to India and China. While it reduces the possibility of a sudden disruption to global oil supplies, the potential for the waiver to be reversed means political and compliance risks would continue to influence trade, shipping and pricing decisions.
"For refiners in India and China, the waiver is really about reducing the uncertainty rather than changing the crude supply picture," said Premasish Das, executive director for oil analytics at S&P Global Energy CERA. "Russian barrels were already flowing into these markets, but the waiver removes an immediate concern and gives refiners more confidence to continue buying grades that make economic sense."
"However, as the legislation remains in place, refiners may still want to keep alternative supply options open in case the situation changes," Das said.
US President Donald Trump signed an executive order Oct. 16, waiving measures included in the Lindsey O. Graham Sanctioning Russia and Iran Act for the largest buyers of Russian oil, citing "national interests," days before they were due to come into effect.
The legislation targeted buyers of Russian hydrocarbons, allowing the president to impose tariffs of up to 100% on goods imported from the five largest buyers of Russian crude oil or natural gas by total volume over the year before enactment, among other measures.
Countries such as India and China can continue to import Russian crude without immediately facing the threat of additional

US penalties or tariffs. However, the waiver does not permanently revoke the law, and it can be withdrawn. As a result, buyers may choose to diversify their oil supplies and remain cautious about future sanctions risks, industry sources said.
"Markets are likely to view the waiver as a tactical reprieve rather than a durable policy settlement," said B. Anand, former CEO of Nayara Energy and founder of consultancy firm Meridian Partners. "Past US decisions not to renew waivers reinforce caution: buyers may use the procurement window, but longerterm commitments will depend on greater confidence in policy continuity."
Russian flows to Asia intact Industry sources said India's demand for Russian crude is
expected to remain strong in the near term. However, refiners would continue to diversify their supply sources as insurance against potential future US actions.
"As of now, it is a sixty-day pause only. So, we have to wait and watch how the tri-lateral deliberations of US, Ukraine and Russia unfold. We can hope for the best," said DLN Sastri, former director of oil refining and marketing at the Federation of Indian Petroleum Industry.
Russian crude discharges to India rose sharply in the first half of 2026, increasing from 1.17 million barrels/day in January to a peak of 2.43 million b/d in June, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data. Flows eased to 2.05 million b/d in July, 1.98 million b/d in August and 1.64 million b/d in September but remained well above January levels.
Russian crude discharges in China -- including pipeline imports -- remained strong but volatile in 2026, according to General Administration of Customs data.
Flows peaked at 2.72 million b/d in February before declining to 2.38 million b/d in March and 1.93 million b/d in May, GAC data showed. Discharges recovered, reaching 2.65 million b/d in August before moderating to 2.43 million b/d in September, according to GAC data. Despite fluctuations, China's Russian crude intake averaged about 2.35 million b/d over JanuarySeptember, GAC data showed, underscoring sustained demand for discounted Russian barrels.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 23, 2026

China is expected to continue importing Russian crude even without the exemption under the Lindsey O. Graham Act, unless Beijing decides to restrict access, according to Chinese industry sources and analysts.
Tariffs on China's Russian oil purchases could reignite trade tensions with the US while doing little to deter such purchases, given the economic appeal of discounted Russian crude for Chinese refiners.
Russian supplies accounted for 30.9% of China's total crude imports in the first nine months of 2026, GAC data showed.
Additional pressure on supplies A Beijing-based policy analyst specializing in China-Russia
relations said the tariffs may prove ineffective and could drive oil prices higher, putting additional pressure on the global economy.
"It would be another round of the tariff war without the exemption, repeating what happened last year," the analyst said.
The price of ESPO cargoes loaded in the Far East fell to ICE Brent plus $20/b on Oct. 23, down from a premium of $30/b a month ago on a DES Shandong basis, due to improving crude supplies in the global market, according to market sources.
China's independent refiners are expected to continue taking advantage of deeply discounted Russian crude that some Indian buyers and Chinese state-owned companies may avoid, even if the US tariff takes effect, industry sources said.
"We do not export anything to the US, and who does not like cheap feedstocks?" said an independent refiner in Dongying, illustrating why China's smaller refiners are relatively insulated from US trade measures.
China's independent refiners have limited financial exposure to international markets and are barred from exporting refined products, making them less vulnerable to US trade measures than globally active state-owned oil companies.
-- Sambit Mohanty, Oceana Zhou
Petronas sets November Malaysian crude OSP at Dated Brent plus $19.50/b
November premium rises $4.45/b MOM
Tapis differential to MCO OSP at $7.96/b discount
State-owned Petronas has set the November Malaysian crude oil official selling price at a premium of $19.50/barrel to Platts Dated Brent crude assessments, up $4.45/b from October, the company said in an Oct. 23 notice.
Petronas also adjusted the price differentials for its secondary crude grades relative to its main crude basket.
The Miri Light differential to the MCO OSP was set at a discount of $4.04/b for November-loading cargoes, compared with a discount of $3.71/b for October, according to the notice.
The company began publishing the price differential for Miri Light crude against the MCO OSP for cargoes loading from October 2025 onward, with the grade remaining in the basket that forms the MCO OSP.

The MCO OSP basket comprises flagship grades, including Labuan, Miri Light, Kikeh and Kimanis crudes.
The Tapis differential to the MCO OSP was set at a discount of $7.96/b for November-loading cargoes, compared with a discount of $7.22/b for October, according to the notice.
The Dulang and Cendor grades were set at premiums of $2.74/b and $2.98/b, respectively, to the MCO OSP for Novemberloading cargoes, compared with premiums of $2.93/b and $2.68/b to the MCO OSP for October.
The Malaysian crude oil OSP differential takes into account a number of factors, such as the average premium or discount to Platts Dated Brent crude assessments in physical spot sales of Labuan, Miri Light, Kikeh and Kimanis grades loading during the month.
Petronas also considers assessments of those grades by price information providers over a specific period, as well as buyers' views on trades and any recommendations they make regarding the value of the crude grades.

SINGAPORE DATA: Crude oil imports rise 69% week over week to 1.14 mil mt
Middle East accounts for 74% of crude oil imports
First Libyan condensate since at least start of 2025
Singapore's crude oil imports rose 69.4% week over week to a nearly three-month high of 1.14 million metric tons over Oct. 15-21, data from Enterprise Singapore showed Oct. 22, with the city-state importing its first Libyan condensate since at least the start of 2025.
The Middle East accounted for 74% of total crude oil imports, with 134,666 mt from Saudi Arabia and 714,529 mt from the UAE, according to the data.
Imports were also recorded at 216,997 mt from Brazil and 75,244 mt from Brunei, the data showed.
Total condensate imports for the week were 210,000 mt, with 150,082 mt from Australia and 59,918 mt from Libya, according to the data. This marked the first Libyan condensate shipment to Singapore since at least the start of 2025, Enterprise Singapore data compiled by Platts showed.

Increase of one cargo month on month

Platts Asia Pacific Sweet Crude Daily Market Analysis
Petronas sets Nov Malaysian crude OSP
Africa lays oil hopes on more tentative exploration pacts
The Asia-Pacific regional complex saw the emergence of Malaysia's official crude price differentials as the trading week inched to a close Oct. 23.
State-owned Petronas has set the November Malaysian crude oil official selling price at a premium of $19.50/barrel to Platts Dated Brent crude assessments, up $4.45/b from October, the company said in an Oct. 23 notice.
The Miri Light differential to the MCO OSP was set at a discount of $4.04/b for November-loading cargoes, compared with a discount of $3.71/b for October, according to the notice.
The Tapis differential to the MCO OSP was set at a discount of

$7.96/b for November-loading cargoes, compared with a discount of $7.22/b for October, according to the notice.
The Dulang and Cendor grades were set at premiums of $2.74/b and $2.98/b, respectively, to the MCO OSP for Novemberloading cargoes, compared with premiums of $2.93/b and $2.68/b to the MCO OSP for October.
Meanwhile, with shifting projections around peak oil and wars in Ukraine and Iran spurring renewed interest in African oil and gas exploration, countries across the continent have been working hard to lure international oil companies to their basins in the past year.
Those efforts were on show again at Africa Energy Week -- a giant annual oil and gas conference in Cape Town over Oct. 12-16 -- with countries from Senegal and Algeria to Namibia and Uganda pitching investors on their upstream opportunities.
Against this backdrop, a new deal type has emerged on the continent: low-commitment Memorandums of Understanding to examine geological data across offshore licenses. And the world's largest oil companies have been using these arrangements to hoover up acreage up and down the continent.
Elsewhere, the US waiver on Russian crude has reduced uncertainty for Indian and Chinese refiners, giving them greater confidence to continue purchasing economically attractive Russian grades while keeping alternative supply options open in case the situation changes, analysts and industry sources said Oct. 22.
The waiver is expected to preserve, rather than materially alter, Russian crude flows to India and China. While it reduces the possibility of a sudden disruption to global oil supplies, the potential for the waiver to be reversed means political and compliance risks would continue to influence trade, shipping and pricing decisions.
"For refiners in India and China, the waiver is really about reducing the uncertainty rather than changing the crude supply picture," said Premasish Das, executive director for oil analytics at S&P Global Energy CERA. "Russian barrels were already flowing into these markets, but the waiver removes an immediate concern and gives refiners more confidence to continue buying grades that make economic sense."

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 23, 2026

A tender offering Dec.-Jan. arrival Khafji crude in the prior Oct. 22 session was heard to have been awarded to four refiners based in China, Taiwan and Singapore, with price levels on an STS Fujairah basis heard at discounts close to $10/b to Platts Dubai, sources said.
Middle East crude differentials were otherwise little changed day over day, with the differential of benchmark Platts cash Dubai against same-month Dubai futures easing by 10 cents/b to a $7.99/b premium at the Oct. 23 Asian close.
This comes even as outright prices and time spreads across the broader oil complex firmed further overnight and into the Oct. 23 session, as traders priced in additional risk premiums amid ongoing attacks on regional infrastructure and shipping.
Latest tanker-tracking data showed tanker transits through the Strait of Hormuz holding up despite the latest uptick in hostilities.
Observed and assumed vessel movements through the Strait of Hormuz increased to 23 on Oct. 22, up from a revised 19 transits recorded on Oct. 21. In contrast, activity at the Bab al-Mandab Strait continued to weaken, with total crossings declining to 27 from 35 a day earlier, according to an Oct. 23 S&P Global Commodities at Sea report.
Middle Eastern oil exports have rebounded to more than 19 million barrels per day by mid-October, Platts earlier reported. On a seven-day moving average basis, Middle Eastern oil exports fell from nearly 21 million b/d on Oct. 1 to 15.2 million b/d Oct. 12 before the latest recovery to 19.2 million b/d on Oct. 18, equivalent to 74% of the pre-war level, according to CAS.
Marcus Baker, global marine head of insurance brokerage Marsh, said additional war risk premiums were rising to 10%12.5% of a ship's hull value for Hormuz passage from 7.5%-12.5% as of Oct. 16.

Oil NWE and Med Weekly Sour Crude Market Analysis
NWE sours under pressure from ample supply
Middle East flows depress Urals DAP India
The European sour complex remained on a bearish trajectory over the week to Oct. 23 as ample supply limited any potential upside to prices.
Improving inflows of Saudi barrels are expected to keep a lid on any potential upside in prices in Europe, compounding an already oversupplied outlook in the segment, traders said.
This was demonstrated through the Platts Market on Close assessment process with BP offering a first-decade November loading cargo over several sessions, but struggled to attract a buyer.
"I'm guessing a lot of Saudi crude is heading to NWE," said one sour crude trader Oct. 23, noting that the barrels are filling key shorts in the region.

"Either way, there should be somewhat of a correction on JS," the trader continued.
Sour barrels in the Mediterranean, however, struggled to rebound from their earlier decline, but appeared to have found a floor.
Basrah Medium cargoes arriving in November were consistently heard offered flat to November average Dated Brent, while Kirkuk was valued slightly higher.
Differentials could face a further downside risk amid increased offering from Saudi Aramco for flows through the Suez Canal, according to market participants.
Tanker traffic through the Suez Canal has risen to the highest in nearly three years on increased Saudi crude flows, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.
Crossings seen in recent weeks were the highest since December 2023, when weekly transits started to collapse from a normal level of 150-160 as Houthi militants launched intense attacks on merchant ships in the Red Sea as part of the IsraelHamas war, Platts reported previously.
Aramco barrels are priced against ICE Brent futures, making them more competitive against crude priced at a differential to Dated Brent. "At the moment, this is the barrel to beat," one Mediterranean source said.
With the punitive freight environment limiting the arbitrage economics of moving Latin American barrels toward Asia, those volumes are pointing toward Europe at competitive levels, traders in both the North Sea and Med have said.
Improved flows from the Middle East have also weighed on Urals, which weakened over the week amid increased competition.
The price for Urals delivered DAP India in December was last heard at around flat to Dated Brent, around $3/b lower week over week.
"Persian Gulf volume is definitely pressuring the prompt market, particularly for India and China," an India-based source said.
While spot prices are lower, traders refrained from making any long-term plans as any escalation in the Middle East could redirect crude flows again.
"It's very difficult to plan anything. When we need to buy, we look at the situation in the market, and we act immediately," the India-based source added.
-- Jan Kedzior, Ernest Puey

Americas Crude
US DOE approves 400,000-barrel SPR exchange for Phillips 66 Lake Charles refinery
Phillips 66 to repay crude plus premium volume
SPR stocks drop to 282.7 million barrels
The US Department of Energy authorized an emergency exchange of up to 400,000 barrels of crude from the Strategic Petroleum Reserve to address a supply disruption affecting Phillips 66's Lake Charles refinery in Louisiana, the department announced Oct. 23.
The crude will be available for immediate delivery from the SPR's West Hackberry site to offset temporary disruptions to refinery crude deliveries and support transportation fuel production for Louisiana and surrounding markets, DOE said in a release.

Phillips 66 will return the borrowed crude along with additional premium barrels.
DOE did not identify the cause or expected duration of the disruption, or specify a repayment schedule or premium volume in its announcement. Spokespeople for DOE and Phillips 66 declined to discuss the specific cause of the refinery disruption.
"Phillips 66 informed DOE that disruptions affecting deliveries through its primary crude oil supply pipeline created an immediate supply challenge for its Lake Charles Refinery," DOE spokesperson Emily Matthews wrote in an email. "The company requested SPR crude to help maintain refinery operations while addressing those supply disruptions."
"We typically do not comment on refinery operations or commercial activity," Phillips 66 spokesperson Al Ortiz wrote in an email. "However, we can confirm we will receive crude oil from the US Strategic Petroleum Reserve. We do not anticipate impacts to Lake Charles operations."
The targeted refinery exchange follows a series of larger SPR solicitations in 2026 under the US commitment to release 172 million barrels as part of a coordinated 400 million-barrel International Energy Agency emergency release.
On Sept. 29, DOE offered up to 40 million barrels from the Big Hill and Bryan Mound sites for delivery in November and December. At the time, the department said five previous solicitations had collectively awarded more than 133 million barrels across four completed exchanges, with earlier exchanges securing a 25% premium in returned barrels. SPR stocks stood at 282.7 million barrels in the week ended Oct. 16, down from 282.9 million barrels a week earlier, according to US Energy Information Administration data.
-- Eamonn Brennan
Argentina's Neuquén sees oil output surge to record high in September
Production surges 26% to 714,089 b/d
Vaca Muerta leads at 97.7% of the total
Gas output rises 9.5% year over year
In Neuquén, Argentina, crude production surged to a record high of 714,089 barrels/day in September from the year-earlier month, led by the Vaca Muerta shale play, while gas rose 9.5% to 104.76 million cubic meters/day over the same period, the province's Energy Ministry said Oct. 23.
Oil production in Neuquén, the country's biggest oil and natural gas province, increased 26% from 566,967 b/d in September 2025, and was up 4.2% from 685,316 b/d in August this year, the ministry said in a statement.
The growth was led by rising output in Vaca Muerta, which produced 697,595 b/d in September, or 97.7% of the province's total crude that month, the ministry said.
The southwestern province's total crude production rose 30% in the first nine months of this year from the same period in 2025, it added.

The month-over-month growth in September was led by increased output in the YPF's Bandurria Sur, Pampa Energía's Rincón de Aranda, YPF's La Angostura Sur I, Vista Energy's Bajada del Palo Oeste, Chevron's El Trapial Este and GeoPark's Loma Jarillosa Este, the ministry said.
Vaca Muerta is driving Argentina's total oil production growth, which rose 15.5% year over year to a record high of 973,700 b/d in September, according to data from the Argentinian Energy Secretariat.
Gas production rises Argentina produced 143.1 MMcm/d of gas in September,
according to the latest data from the national Energy Secretariat. Neuquén's gas production rose 9.5% to 104.76 MMcm/d in
September from 95.71 MMcm/d in the year-earlier month, taking the cumulative year-over-year rise in the first nine months of 2026 to 6.1%.
Of the gas produced in September, Vaca Muerta accounted for 85.97 MMcm/d, or 82.1% of the total, while another 9.75 MMcm/d came from tight gas fields and the rest from conventional reserves, the ministry said.
However, September's gas production was down 9.3% from 115.47 MMcm/d in August, according to ministry records.
The biggest month-over-month production drops in September came from Tecpetrol's Fortín de Piedra, Pampa Energía's Sierra Chata and Pluspetrol's La Calera, the ministry said, adding that the month-over-month decline stems largely from seasonal factors, as rising temperatures reduced residential demand for heating.
Vaca Muerta, in northern Patagonia, is expected to lead a rise in Argentina's oil output to 1.7 million b/d in 2030, while gas is expected to increase to 281 MMcm/d, according to a forecast from the Argentinian Chamber of Hydrocarbons Exploration and Production, an industry group.

Gasoline
European gasoline forward cracks converge amid diminishing blend value
Gasoline cracks compress across forward curve
Gasoline-naphtha spreads converge as blending incentive eases
European gasoline cracks converged sharply across the prompt forward curve over the week ended Oct. 23, while elevated freight costs have made it increasingly expensive to clear barrels from Northwest Europe.
The M1 and M2 FOB ARA Eurobob cracks moved closer over the week, with the M1 crack at a 99 cents/b premium to M2 on Oct. 22, narrowing by $3.56/b week over week, according to data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
Further along the curve, a contango structure continued to emerge. The M2/M3 FOB ARA crack spread stood at a 28 cents/b discount on Oct. 22, while the M3/M4 spread was at a $1.51/b discount, Platts data showed.
The convergence came amid weakness in European gasoline flat prices, with the backwardated prompt market continuing to incentivize holders to sell. At the same time, elevated freight costs have made it increasingly expensive to clear barrels from Northwest Europe, adding pressure to FOB export economics.
"Freight [is] becoming a barrier to doing stuff. It's expensive to go anywhere," a Northwest European gasoline trader said Oct. 23.
Platts assessed the cross-Channel UKC-UKC 22,000 mt clean tanker freight rate at $37.45/mt on Oct. 22, up 26.1% day over day and 78.2% month over month.
Trans-Atlantic freight has also risen, with the UKC-USAC 37,000 mt clean tanker rate at $31.31/mt on Oct. 22, up 30.7% month over month.

The trader said the trans-Atlantic arbitrage appeared more favorable further along the curve but remained closed in the prompt, with any reopening dependent on freight costs easing.
ARA was meanwhile seeing additional cargoes moving into the region, the trader said, while elevated freight costs limited the ability to clear barrels toward the Mediterranean or across the Atlantic.
Despite the weakness, the prompt gasoline market remained backwardated, providing an incentive to sell barrels as quickly as possible while demand remained available, the trader said. This could support further stock draws, although the trader expected cash differentials to weaken as the market moves further into winter.
Gasoline-naphtha spreads converge Gasoline-naphtha spreads have undergone a similar
convergence along the forward curve, with the prompt premium over later months narrowing sharply over the week.
The M1 gasoline-naphtha spread was $143.50/mt on Oct. 22, $3.25/mt above M2, with the premium narrowing by $25.25/mt week over week, Platts data showed. M1 was $1.50/mt above M3, with that premium narrowing by $33.50/mt over the same period.
The compression marks a significant retreat from the record highs previously seen in gasoline-naphtha spreads, reducing the incentive to pull naphtha into the gasoline blending pool.
"In the last week, we've seen some softness, but still volumes being pulled in the gasoline pool. Spread-wise, the incentive to put [naphtha] in gasoline has become a bit less just because of the economics," a trading source said.
With seasonal gasoline demand easing into winter, market participants expect the petrochemical sector to regain influence over European naphtha pricing.
"[In the] winter season [...] the pricing on naphtha is more driven by [the] petchem complex," the trading source said.
Rising propane prices have also reduced propane's competitiveness as a petrochemical feedstock, supporting an increase in petrochemical demand for naphtha over the past week and providing an additional source of support to naphtha relative to gasoline.

Gasoline inventories in the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp hub declined 14.41% to 707,000 metric tons in the week ended Oct. 22, marking a significant draw and hitting its lowest since Sept. 9, 2021, according to Insights Global data.
European gasoline prices have been declining amid a seasonal lull in demand and high freight rates across the region, limiting

barrel mobility and restricting export demand, sources said. "Freight is the biggest hurdle at the moment. Demurrage
is expensive as well, [and] delays are costly despite softer backwardation," an European trader said, adding that the broader market weakness reflected a combination of high freight costs and falling demand.
According to data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, FOB AR Eurobob barges recovered from a two-month low on Oct. 22, rising $13.50/mt day over day but down 7.69% week over week to $1,098.75/mt.
Part of the destocking seen across Northwest European markets was incentivized by a severe backwardation structure over the week, prompting higher trading activity in the Platts Market on Close assessment process over the one-week period, with liquidity narrowing at the back end of the week.
As cash differentials have come off significantly and inventories have been drawn down, sources expect a slowdown in the rate of depreciation in market value.
The physical premium for FOB AR Eurobob barges over the equivalent November swap was at $54/mt on Oct. 22, narrowing $74/mt week over week.
While cash differentials were at a near four-week low, market participants raised concerns about narrowing margins amid the ongoing crude strength.
"Landed values [are] expensive for crude, [and] cracks cannot give up anymore from where they stand at around $19/b, unless crude softens," a Mediterranean analyst said.
Platts assessed the front-month FOB ARA Eurobob barge crack at $18.50/b on Oct. 22, down 15 cents/b day over day and $5.50/b week over week.
Meanwhile, a tightening supply picture in the Mediterranean sees some regional traders rallying expectations that renewed demand for ARA-Med arbitrages will emerge across the first half of November, though this depends on freight cooling, sources said.
"Med is getting tighter indeed, [and we] need to pull product from the ARA," a second European trader said. Eurobob had been selling off, making spreads more attractive, the trader added, "but [they] need to grow significantly more to make the arb happen."
A third European trader similarly said the Mediterranean was "running tight in product" and would "not cope without additional inflows from outside," although high freight costs remained an obstacle.
The November Med/North gasoline differential, the spread between the November FOB Med swaps and the equivalent FOB ARA Eurobob barge, was up $1.25/mt at a $21/mt premium, with the December spread 50 cents/mt wider at a $22/mt premium.
"Beginning of November [is] very tight," another trading source said, adding that there were "less and less avails" while "more and more people [are] asking for more barrels."

Oct. 22 to 363,000 mt, Insights Global data showed.

Platts European Gasoline Daily Market Analysis
ARA gasoline inventories drop 14% to lowest since 2021
High freight costs limit export opportunities
Prompt crack spreads compress amid destocking
European gasoline markets strengthened Oct. 23, supported by falling regional inventories, while elevated freight costs continued to restrict the movement of barrels and weigh on export economics.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed FOB AR Eurobob gasoline barges up $30.75/metric ton at $1,129.50/mt on Oct. 23, while the equivalent cash differential narrowed 50 cents/mt to $53.50/mt.
Gasoline inventories in the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp hub fell 14.41% to 707,000 metric tons in the week ended Oct. 22, their lowest level since Sept. 9, 2021, according to Insights Global data.
The stock draw came against a backdrop of softer seasonal gasoline demand and high freight costs, which have constrained barrel mobility and limited export opportunities, market sources said.
"Freight is the biggest hurdle at the moment. Demurrage is expensive as well, [and] delays are costly despite softer backwardation," a European trader said, adding that broader market weakness reflected a combination of high freight costs and declining demand.
A steeply backwardated prompt structure had encouraged destocking across Northwest Europe during the week, while trading activity in the Platts Market on Close assessment process was stronger earlier in the period before liquidity thinned toward the end of the week.

At the same time, European gasoline cracks compressed sharply across the prompt forward curve in the week, according to Platts analysis on Oct. 23. The M1 FOB ARA Eurobob crack was at a 71 cents/b premium to M2 on Oct. 23, with the spread narrowing by $3.29/b week over week, according to Platts data.
Further forward, the curve moved into contango. The M2/M3 FOB ARA crack spread was at a 61 cents/b discount on Oct. 23, while M3/M4 stood at a $2.06/b discount.
The flattening of the prompt crack structure coincided with weaker European gasoline flat prices, with the remaining backwardation continuing to encourage holders to release prompt barrels. Meanwhile, high freight costs made clearing surplus material from Northwest Europe increasingly expensive, adding pressure to FOB export economics.
"Freight [is] becoming a barrier to doing stuff. It's expensive to go anywhere," a Northwest European gasoline trader said Oct. 23.
Platts assessed the front-month FOB AR Eurobob barge at $1,076/mt, up $31.25/mt on the day, with the equivalent crack down 63 cents/b at $17.87/b.
The November/December spread rose $1.75/mt to $42/mt, while December/January also gained $1.75/mt to $23.75/mt.
The November Mediterranean/North gasoline differential, representing the spread between the November FOB Mediterranean swap and the equivalent FOB ARA Eurobob barge, was unchanged at a $21/mt premium. The December differential was also unchanged, at a $22/mt premium.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Friedrich Gutsche

Russian diesel tanker heads to New York as Kremlin claims regular shipments
Malta-flagged tanker should carry 40,000 mt fuel
Agioi Fanendes left Primorsk Oct. 22: CAS
US lifts sanctions, enables Russian imports
The Malta-flagged medium-sized tanker Agioi Fanendes is heading to New York carrying Russian diesel, according to S&P Global Energy Commodities at Sea data, with the Kremlin expecting deliveries to continue "on a regular basis."
The Kremlin's spokesperson, Dmitry Peskov, said Oct. 23 that the first tanker carrying Russian diesel was sent to the US.
"The first tanker has, in fact, set off toward its destinations -- to its buyers -- and [subsequent shipments] will continue on a regular basis," Peskov told journalists, according to TASS. He did not clarify the name of the vessel sent to the US or who the buyers of the delivery are.
The Agioi Fanendes tanker left Russia's port of Primorsk on Oct. 22 and is expected to reach New York around Nov. 6-7, according to CAS data. It is unclear who the buyer and the seller of the vessel might be.
The first diesel tanker from Russia to the US since 2022 is expected to carry about 40,000 metric tons, Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak said Oct. 21.
Eastern Mediterranean Maritime, which manages the Agioi Fanendes, according to its website, could not be reached for comment Oct. 23.

While the vessel remains in the Baltic Sea, it can still divert its destination.
Market reaction The delivery of these diesel volumes to the US "would only be
a displacement of volume from Brazil," according to a European diesel trader who asked for anonymity as not authorized to speak publicly. "It is a logistical change regarding where the product goes, rather than an increase in flows. We would have to see vastly more flows for it to [affect] the European market."
Shipping market participants were also seeing signs of additional Russian-loading opportunities. A Greece-based broker said UAN and clean petroleum product stems were emerging, with New York, Houston and Canada's Come By Chance among potential destinations, although fixtures had yet to be confirmed.
The emergence of further Russian-loading stems could also have implications for the European MR tanker market. Shipping sources said additional Russian business could draw vessels away from conventional European employment, while a larger pool of owners willing to lift Russian cargoes could put downward pressure on freight rates for Russian-loading voyages.
Russia was the second-largest diesel exporter globally before a major fuel crisis began in the country this summer, triggered by Ukrainian attacks on Russian refineries. Turkey, Brazil and Egypt were the main buyers of Russian flows in the past years after the EU banned Russian fuel products in response to the country's full-scale invasion of Ukraine.
The US Treasury Department's Office of Foreign Assets Control granted on Oct. 9 a sanctions waiver to Russia allowing the sale, delivery and import of Russian-origin diesel fuel, including into the US. In response, the Russian government has partially lifted its full diesel export ban introduced in July.
Ukraine's President Volodymyr Zelenskyy has been under pressure from US President Donald Trump to stop attacks on Russian refineries. The last time Ukraine struck a Russian refinery was on Oct. 15, marking a slowdown in Ukraine's campaign targeting Russian energy infrastructure.
It remains unclear how much additional diesel Russia can export to global markets in the coming months.
Novak said Oct. 9 that Russia is ready to export 300,000 mt of diesel in October, 500,000 mt in November and 1 million mt in December. He later said that Russian diesel deliveries can reach up to 3.5 million mt/month by the end of 2026, without clarifying the discrepancy between the figures.
Zelenskyy said Oct. 15 that US military officials told their counterparts that if Ukraine did not stop the energy strikes, the US would block intelligence sharing with Ukraine. But Ukraine is only ready to stop its attacks on Russian refineries if Russia stops striking its energy infrastructure and killing Ukrainians, Zelenskyy said.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the European diesel benchmark ULSD 10 ppm CIF NWE Cargo price at $1,479 / mt on Oct. 22, up from $1,470.5/mt on Oct. 22, but still well below

Diesel and gasoil stocks in the Amsterdam-RotterdamAntwerp refining hub declined 0.45% to 1.774 million metric tons in the week ended Oct. 22, the latest data from Insights Global showed. The premium-grade fuel continues to trade at elevated levels as supply constraints persist across the European market.
The European supply crunch has been exacerbated by weak trans-Atlantic arbitrage incentives and the local prioritization of jet fuel production amid a negative diesel-jet regrade in Europe.
The US Gulf Coast-Europe diesel arbitrage stood closed at minus 11 cents/b on Oct. 21, according to Platts data, discouraging flows across the Atlantic. Meanwhile, the physical AmsterdamRotterdam-Antwerp diesel crack was at $73.66/b Oct. 21, implying a $13.68/b regrade in favor of jet fuel output.
Similarly, the 50 ppm gasoil barge price gained $15.25/mt to reach $1,424.25/mt Oct. 22, marking a 1.08% increase on the day. The 50 ppm gasoil specification, used as heating fuel in Switzerland and Germany, has maintained firm pricing as the heating season approaches.
Sources have said that Europe's heating oil demand season is beginning to pick up heading into winter; however, buyers seem to be holding back on seasonal buying appetite as gasoil flat prices have roughly doubled from a year earlier.
For comparison, the flat price of 50 ppm gasoil barges stood at $745.75/mt Feb. 27, the day before the Middle East war started.

Platts European Diesel Daily Market Analysis
European diesel prices surge on Middle East risks
Russian tanker departs for US
The European diesel complex extended its gains Oct. 23, amid bullish market sentiment over risks to oil operations with escalations in the Middle East.
Platts assessed the front-month ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract up $45/metric ton to $1,492.25/mt Oct.23, the highest level seen in the market since Oct. 8.
Oil prices have climbed as renewed Houthi attacks raise risks to Saudi Arabia's oil infrastructure and the export routes the kingdom has increasingly relied on amid disruption to shipping through the Strait of Hormuz.
Saudi crude continues to reach international markets via Yanbu, the Suez Canal and Egypt's SUMED pipeline, but the latest attacks have put the market on edge over both production infrastructure and alternative export routes out of the Middle East.
"The threat is no longer one route or one front: Iran, the Houthis in Yemen and militias in Iraq can all put Middle East oil infrastructure at risk," according to the latest crude oil market outlook from S&P Global Energy CERA.
Moreover, the first diesel tanker from Russia to the US since 2022 departed Oct. 23, but has done little to alleviate pressure on the global diesel market.
The Malta-flagged medium-sized tanker Agioi Fanendes is heading to New York carrying Russian diesel, according to S&P Global Energy Commodities at Sea data, with the Kremlin

expecting deliveries to continue "on a regular basis." The Kremlin's spokesperson, Dmitry Peskov, said Oct. 23 that
the first tanker carrying Russian diesel was sent to the US. "The first tanker has, in fact, set off toward its destinations
-- to its buyers -- and [subsequent shipments] will continue on a regular basis," Peskov told journalists, according to state-owned news agency TASS. He did not clarify the name of the vessel sent to the US or who the buyers of the delivery are.
The Agioi Fanendes tanker left Russia's port of Primorsk Oct. 22 and is expected to reach New York around Nov. 6-7, according to CAS data. It is unclear who the buyer and the seller of the vessel might be.
This comes after the US Treasury Department's Office of Foreign Assets Control granted Oct. 9, a sanctions waiver to Russia allowing the sale, delivery and import of Russian-origin diesel fuel, including into the US.
However, market participants are doubtful that Russian exports to the US will bring any material change to product availability or prices in Europe.
The delivery of these diesel volumes to the US "would only be a displacement of volume from Brazil," according to a Europebased diesel trader. "It is a logistical change regarding where the product goes, rather than an increase in flows. We would have to see vastly more flows for it to [affect] the European market."
The trader added that "the product was not coming to Europe anyway," highlighting the perceived minimal impact of the news.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Sophia Aung
Marine Fuel
Q3 oil bunker sales rise in Antwerp, Rotterdam while LNG and biofuels falter
LNG pricing surge triggers fuel switching
Biofuel sales drop as pooling costs decline
Antwerp on track to overtake Rotterdam
Bunker fuel sales strengthened at Antwerp-Bruges and Rotterdam in the third quarter, according to data from the port authorities, with stronger demand for oil-based fuels offsetting lower deliveries of LNG and biofuels.
The development came as a surge in LNG price made the fuel less competitive, while shipping companies with conventional ships could meet compliance requirements via pooling arrangements at lower costs than using biofuels.
Total bunker sales at Antwerp-Bruges rose 5.3% quarter on quarter to 2.158 million metric tons in Q3, the highest in seven quarters, the Belgian port complex's authority said in an Oct. 22 report.
Very low sulfur fuel oil sales rose 25.4% from Q2 to 674,713 mt, while high sulfur fuel oil sales increased 7.3% to 630,706 mt. Ultra low sulfur fuel oil sales slipped 3.2% to 159,663 mt and gasoil sales fell 4.7% to 348,957 mt.

SINGAPORE DATA: Heavy distillate stocks rebound 4.5% to four-week high
Imports rise 75% week over week
Middle Eastern inflows nearly double
Exports from Singapore up 226%
Singapore's commercial stockpiles of heavy distillates rebounded 4.5% to 18.858 million barrels in the week ended Oct. 21, following six consecutive weeks of inventory draws, Enterprise Singapore data showed Oct. 22.
However, stocks were 18.1% lower in the week ended Oct. 21 than in the corresponding week a year earlier, the data also showed.
Weekly residual fuel stocks in Singapore have averaged 20.709 million barrels so far in 2026, compared with a weekly average of 22.8 million barrels in 2025 and 19.7 million barrels in 2024, according to Enterprise Singapore data.

The weekly build in Singapore's inventories was partly driven by a 74.9% week-over-week increase in imports, which totaled 965,469 mt in the week ended Oct. 21.
About 44.3% of the imported volume came from within Asia, with Indonesia and India supplying 77,144 mt and 63,765 mt of heavy distillates, respectively. Arrivals from Thailand jumped to 116,665 mt from 31,926 mt in the preceding week.
Imports from the Middle East -- the UAE, Syria and Saudi Arabia -- increased 91.1% week over week to 297,519 mt in the week ended Oct. 21.
Singapore also saw 64,920 mt of arrivals from the Netherlands, after receiving no European inflows for two weeks.
Meanwhile, fuel oil exports from Singapore more than tripled from the preceding week to 440,316 mt in the week ended Oct. 21.
Of the total, 208,378 mt was bound for China, up from 84,062 mt the week before.
Singapore's inventory data covers only stocks at onshore terminals. Enterprise Singapore describes heavy distillates as "residues," which include cracked and straight-run fuel oil and low-sulfur waxy residue.
In the downstream market, spot trading discussions at the Singapore bunker hub were steady in the week to Oct. 22, with healthy supply and demand noted for November, according to trade sources.
Firmer cargo prices on Oct. 22 raised concerns that premiums could rise, prompting last-minute buying for October deliveries, two Singapore-based traders said.
Limited barging slots for the rest of October kept premiums elevated, with mostly pocket slots available. Additional offers for October deliveries hinged on vessel schedules, with delays potentially freeing up more supply, a Singapore-based trader said.
Platts assessed the Singapore-delivered 0.5%S marine fuel bunker premium over benchmark FOB Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S cargo prices at $86.02/mt on Oct. 22, up 38% week over week and the highest in about two months.
Platts assessed the Singapore-delivered 380 CST HSFO bunker premium over FOB Singapore 380 CST HSFO cargo prices at $95.97/mt, up 11% week over week and the highest in about seven months.
Premiums for low-sulfur fuel oil ex-wharf cargoes were steady to slightly firmer Oct. 22, with first-half November loadings heard traded from the low $40s/mt to $45/mt, according to trade sources.
-- Iris Leung, Nur syamieza Ahmad zubir

Platts European Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
ARA fuel oil stocks decrease 0.9% to 930,000 mt VLSFO exports rise on Americas, Asia premiums High freight costs limit HSFO imports from Americas

Very low sulfur fuel oil product moved out of Europe Oct. 23, while freight rates weighed on HSFO imports and Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp stocks slipped week over week.
In the Northwest European fuel oil market, ARA stocks fell 0.9% to 930,000 metric tons in the week to Oct. 22, according to Insights Global data, after building the previous week.
In the European VLSFO market, higher flat prices weighed on end-user buying appetite. However, elevated premiums in the Americas and Asian markets have led to volume flowing out of Europe on arbitrage routes, thereby supporting prices.
Overall, however, there was ample availability of blend stocks such as low sulfur straight run, where a well-supplied market had pushed blenders to sell straight run product into the VLSFO bunkering pool, participants said.
"The market is swimming in LSSR," said a trader. In other news, Cyprus utility demand had helped support the European LSFO market, with a second trader noting movement of product from Northwest Europe into the Mediterranean to supply utility demand. In the high sulfur fuel oil market, participants described fundamentals as "balanced." However, there were some resupply concerns centered around the closed arbitrage window from the Americas region. "The arb from the Americas is dynamic, differentials in Europe are high, but so is freight. Not that many cargoes are coming to Europe," said another European trader. Another source said that "[HSFO] blending is more challenging" in the Mediterranean. There were no trades in the MOC for fuel oil cargoes. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.

Gas Liquids
North American LPG prices hit multiyear highs amid strong domestic, export demand
US propane exports above 2 million b/d on average in October
US propane inventories hit lowest since August
Edmonton butane prices reach all-time Platts high
North American LPG prices reached multiyear highs in late October, driven by robust domestic demand across the continent

and strong exports to regions such as the Far East. Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed US Gulf Coast
propane prices at $1.0925/gallon on Oct. 22. This marked one of the highest prices since September 2022, while the propane-to-crude ratio was about 46%, on par with a year ago.
The Mont Belvieu propane-to-crude ratio at Energy Transfer consolidated at 47% after rising steadily over the past month. The Enterprise propane ratio was 46%. Both ratios are now in line with 2025.
The rising Gulf Coast propane price comes as propane exports out of the US have held above 2 million barrels/day over the last four weeks, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea shipping data.
In October, Japan was the largest importer, accounting for about 27% of all US propane loadings, with China following at 17%.
This steady export trend was part of the reason US propane and propylene inventories fell below 106 million barrels for the first time since early August during the week ended Oct. 16. Stocks tightened by 500,000 barrels to 105.846 million barrels, their lowest level since early August, according to US Energy Information Administration data released Oct. 21 for the week ended Oct. 16.
The US Gulf Coast propane price has also affected propane prices further north in Canada. Platts assessed October-delivery propane in Edmonton up 1.375 cent/gal to 96.375 cents/gal, according to Platts data. November deliveries also gained, rising 1.875 cent/gal to 96.125 cents/gal, narrowing the prompt-toforward spread.
Stronger domestic, export butane demand North American butane prices, meanwhile, have remained
bullish, particularly since the start of the US shift to wintergrade gasoline, which was pushed forward two weeks to Sept. 1. Butane is typically used as a blendstock in higher-RVP gasoline blending.
Platts assessed butane prices at the Enterprise terminal at $1.785/gal on Oct. 22, marking one of the highest levels since March 2022. The Conway-Enterprise butane price spread, meanwhile, was over 10 cents, compared with less than a cent a year ago.
The Conway-Enterprise price disparity has shown that blenders in the New York Harbor and smaller regional markets are facing very thin margins due to high rail and truck delivery costs, S&P Global Energy CERA analysts said in their Oct. 22 US NGLs report.
That's been part of the reason butane demand in Canada is currently up for use in the butane-tight US Midwest region, as reflected in Western Canadian prices.
Edmonton field-grade butane held firm relative to crude, remaining at 72.50% of the October WTI CMA for prompt deliveries, while November was also steady at 67.50% of the WTI CMA. The absolute differential, however, widened by 1.27 cents/ gal to $1.66/gal on Oct. 22.

Platts Western Mediterranean LPG Daily Commentary
Butane outperforms propane amid tight supply
US exports face Asia competition for cargoes
Mild weather delays European heating demand
The West Mediterranean LPG market ended the week Oct. 23 with tight global supply, stretched shipping and a reshuffling of long-haul flows outweighing lackluster regional demand
Butane stayed the stronger grade, while propane traded in a more balanced market as heating demand was slow to arrive.
Middle East supply disruptions kept the wider LPG balance tight. Disruptions to Middle East supply and shipping lifted demand for barrels sourced outside the region, and that pull was felt most in butane.
US exports remained the key swing source, but the transatlantic arbitrage into the Mediterranean faced stiff competition. The Middle East conflict widened the US-to-Asia arbitrage window, drawing Gulf Coast cargoes east and thinning the pool of spot US barrels available to Mediterranean and North African buyers.
Elevated freight also weighed on the northern route. Coaster movements from Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp and the UK into Morocco remained costly, which kept North African demand anchored to Mediterranean supply.
End-user demand offered limited support. Mild October weather across Europe delayed the seasonal pickup in heating and cylinder offtake.
Petrochemical appetite also remained subdued, with European olefins producers contending with weak underlying demand and ample supply.
Crackers, therefore, had little incentive to step in for Mediterranean LPG. North African import demand, chiefly Moroccan butane, was steady but largely covered through term and contract volumes, which limited spot inquiries.
Participants expected butane to remain well supported into November as blending and cylinder demand build and as Middle East disruptions keep competition for Atlantic Basin barrels intense.
Propane's direction was seen hinging on the arrival of colder weather and on whether US cargoes return to the Mediterranean as the eastbound arbitrage narrows.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the West Mediterranean FOB butane market at $1,246/metric ton, up $60/ mt day over day and up 1 percentage point versus naphtha at 128%, or at a $39/mt discount to FOB Northwest Europe coasters.
Platts assessed coaster freight from Lavera to Mohammedia at $66/mt, steady day over day, based on market information received throughout the day.
The CIF Mediterranean butane large cargo market was assessed at $1,121/mt, or 115.25% of naphtha.
Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB propane market at $954.50/mt, up $24.50/mt day over day.

The CIF Med propane large cargo market was assessed at $849.75/mt, or at a $17.75/mt premium to CIF Northwest Europe large cargoes.
In Morocco, Platts calculated the CIF very large cargo freight forward at $987.50/mt for delivery 45-60 days forward, up $8/mt.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Baran Serdaroglu

Hormuz traffic rises while Bab al-Mandab transits slow

Bab al-Mandab drops to 27, lowest since Sep
Three tankers attacked near Iraq and Oman
Observed and inferred vessel movements through the Strait of Hormuz rose to 23 on Oct. 22, from a revised 19 a day earlier, while Bab al-Mandab Strait crossings fell to 27 from 35, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 23 report.
Hormuz traffic comprised five bulk carriers, four product tankers, four VLCCs, one LPG carrier, one container ship, two cargo vessels and six vessels from other segments, CAS said.
Traffic was broadly balanced by direction, with 11 inbound and 12 outbound movements. Nine vessels were assessed as Iranlinked, while the remaining 14 were classified as compliant, CAS data showed.
Traffic across the US naval blockade line fell to 44 transits on Oct. 22 from 65 a day earlier, the lowest daily count since Oct. 12. Tanker movements across the line dropped to 25, also the lowest since Oct. 12.
"While the decline may reflect softer identifiable traffic, it could also indicate a greater share of activity being conducted without AIS visibility across the Arabian Sea," CAS analysts said. Total movements comprised 24 inbound passages toward the Gulf of Oman and 20 outbound passages.
As of Oct. 23, weekly transits were 114 outbound and 127 inbound, compared with 25 outbound and 129 inbound the previous week, according to UK Maritime Trade Operations. Overall activity was about 70% below pre-conflict levels. AISobserved transits rose to 47 from 42 the previous week, while US-reported facilitated transits were broadly unchanged, UKMTO said.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the freight rate for carrying 270,000 mt of crude from the Persian Gulf to China at $234.93/mt on Oct. 23, down $2.08/mt on the day. The rate for carrying 65,000 mt of refined products from the Gulf to the UKContinent was unchanged at $233.85/mt.
At the Bab al-Mandab Strait, total traffic fell to 27 transits, the lowest daily level since Sept. 16, CAS said. The count included nine tankers, down from 11 a day earlier and the lowest tanker tally since Oct. 11. There were 11 inbound and 16 outbound transits, CAS data showed.

The International Maritime Organization also confirmed that two more vessels were targeted Oct. 20. The product tanker Soyia was damaged about 30 nautical miles southeast of Al Faw, Iraq, while the Kuwaiti Long Range 2 tanker Al Dasma was attacked in the Strait of Hormuz.
The Hong Kong, China-flagged VLCC Chloe was identified as damaged Oct. 19, about 121 nautical miles off Oman, CAS said. Vessel-tracking data showed the laden tanker had exited the Red Sea through the Bab al-Mandab and was carrying Russian crude to India when the incident occurred.

Platts East of Suez Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
MR tankers reposition to Asia for higher pay
LR rates expected to drop on weak demand
East of Suez Medium Range, or MRs were mostly steady Oct. 23 with several tankers ballasting from Africa to Southeast Asia in their quest for higher earnings, market participants said.
More than a dozen MRs are moving towards Singapore, after discharging their cargoes in Africa, said a chartering executive with a global commodities trading company.
The returns are higher for loading cargoes in Southeast Asia for delivery Down Under, compared with Oman or West Coast India loadings for East and South Africa delivery, said a source with a clean oil tanker owner.
The daily scrubber-fitted MR earnings on the benchmark Singapore-Australia are close to $67,000-$77,000, basis round voyage, assuming high sulfur fuel oil bunker prices of around $875/mt, according to the estimates of brokers.
The corresponding daily earnings on the West Coast India/ Oman to East Africa route are at around $57,000-$58,000, assuming HSFO bunker prices of around $730/mt, the brokers said. For older MRs, these earnings are even lower, at around $54,000, the brokers added.
The Great Thita, which was ballasted from Durban, was placed on subjects at $4.85 million by Total for early November diesel loading on the Quanzhou-South Africa route, brokers said. The Seaways Lily, which ballasted from South Africa, was snapped up for an end-October short cross-harbor voyage in Singapore at $1.075 million by Petco, brokers added.
However, the demand for MRs has picked up in the Middle East and several among them, such as, the Virgo, the Torm Torino, the Torm Sublime, the T Jungfrau, the Imperial and the Largo Atom have been placed on subjects but no details were available, sources said. Lesser supply amid strong demand can support freight rates in the near term, they said.
No LR1 cargoes for November loading were heard and with plenty of vessels available for employment, rates are expected to fall for this segment, a second LR broker said. Outbound cargo movement from the Strait of Hormuz could be hampered, leading to a lack of cargoes on the market, a LR broker said.
In a recent fixture, a LR2, the Stresa, was placed on subjects

at w485 by Reliance for Oct. 28 loading on the Sikka-Australia route, sources said. A LR broker said that because this route sees a premium over Sikka-Japan and features a prompt loading date, next-done transactions could fall below this level.
A LR1, the Gem No. 3 was placed on subjects at w500 by Trafigura, for Nov. 2 diesel loading on the Duqm-East Africa route, sources said. The Hafnia Excellence was placed on subjects at w525 by OQ, for Nov. 2 loading on the Sohar-Tamatave route, they said.
-- Sameer Mohindru, Ellie Lin
Platts East of Suez Dirty Tanker Daily Commentary
Gulf of Oman VLCC rates fall below w600
Suezmax, Aframax markets face further downward pressure
The East of Suez VLCC market weakened further Oct. 23, with rates for Gulf of Oman loadings slipping below w600. However, the speed and scale of the decline prompted market participants to question whether similarly steep falls could continue.
"I don't think rates will fall much further. There may be some further declines, but they are unlikely to be as fast or as steep," said a VLCC owner.
Another VLCC owner said the rate would depend on individual owners, with some willing to follow rates lower while others could resist.
Cargoes were heard to have been covered quietly, with AMCL and COSCO ships reportedly put on subjects by a Persian Gulf supplier and Shell for the loading window of end-October to Nov. 5 at w550-575.
The latest reported fixture was at w575, while some market participants believed w550 could not readily be repeated.
Meanwhile, owners loading from Yanbu were heard to have secured rates above the last reported level.
Fixture-wise, Atokos was heard to be put on subjects by Pertamina to load 270,000 mt crude from Yanbu to Cilacap Oct. 30 at a lumpsum of $52.5 million.
Market sources also said four Middle East-loading cargoes were expected to enter the market Oct. 23.
Market sources said reports of further attacks this week along the passage from inside the Persian Gulf to the Gulf of Oman had reinforced the risk premium for inside-Gulf loadings, helping rates hold up better than those for Gulf of Oman loadings.
The Suezmax market was expected by market sources to come under further pressure as VLCC rates continued to decline.
Aframax rates also edged lower amid a slowdown in cargo inquiries this week.
Fresh cargoes emerged Oct. 23, with Vietsea heard seeking an Aframax for loading of 80,000 mt of vacuum gasoil Nov. 3 from Singapore to Dung Quat, while Vitol was heard seeking an Aframax for loading Nov. 6 from Kimanis to Geelong.
Australian loading rates also remained under pressure. "Charterers won't be particularly keen to take Aframaxes for condensate at the moment as LR2s are cheaper," a broker said.

Platts West of Suez Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
Cross UKC handy rates firm
Mixed sentiment across markets
The West of Suez clean tanker market experienced mixed sentiment Oct. 23. The Mediterranean market softened across sizes, while UK Continent firmed on the day.
Market participants described cross-Mediterranean handy size market as quiet. A Europe-based source said, "Handy sizes are softening, the list is a bit longer than usual in West Med."
Following the indications gathered, Platts assessed crossMediterranean, basis 30,000 mt, at w525, down by w10 from Oct. 22.
Black Sea-to-Med shipments, basis 30,000 mt, were assessed at w545, down by w10 from Oct. 22.
The Med-USAC route, basis 37,000 mt, was assessed at w225, unchanged from Oct. 22, and the Med-WAF route, basis 37,000 mt, was assessed at w325, down by w10 from Oct. 22.
A London-based source said the UK Continent market is very busy, with rates firming.
Platts assessed UKC-USAC, basis 37,000 metric tons, at w220, up by w17.5 from Oct.22.
Cross-UKC and Baltic-to-UKC shipments, basis 30,000 mt, were assessed at w500 and w510, respectively, up by w90 from Oct.22.
Platts assessed the WAF-UKC, basis 37,000 metric tons, at w325, up by w30 from Oct. 22. A Europe based broker said WAF position list is tight and lots of vessels are being delayed. "In WAF, there is congestion in most ports, lots of vessels are waiting berthing or STS schedules," the source said.
Platts assessed Med-to-Japan shipments via the Suez Canal, basis 60,000 mt, at $7.3 million, down by $1m from Oct. 22. The 80,000 mt route, via the Suez Canal, was assessed at $12 million, down by $500,000 from Oct. 22.
UKC-West Africa shipments, basis 60,000 mt, were assessed at w295, unchanged from Oct. 22.
-- Alara Tizer
Platts West of Suez VLCC and Suezmax Dirty Tanker Daily Commentary
VLCC rates fall but sentiment steadies on renewed activity
Suezmax rates climb in West Africa, Black Sea
The West of Suez dirty tanker market saw VLCC rates out of West Africa fall Oct. 23, though sentiment began to steady as previously unreported fixtures came to light. Meanwhile, Suezmax rates in both West Africa and the Black Sea pushed higher on firm fundamentals.

Platts assessed freight on the 260,000 mt West Africa-East route at w562.5, down w52.5 day over day.
Despite the lower assessment, market sentiment found a more stable footing as a clearer picture of underlying activity emerged. Shipowners were looking to book vessels early next week to build on this newfound resistance.
In fresh business, HOB was heard to have booked the VL Bright for a 270,000 mt voyage from Sidi Kerir to South Korea for a Nov. 27 laycan, although the rate was not disclosed.
In the West African Suezmax market, much of the business was conducted privately, with few public cargo offerings and charterers showing little urgency to secure vessels.
However, market fundamentals appeared firm. A charterer in Dubai noted that tightness in the East would prevent the market from softening, while a London-based broker attributed the wide range of discussed rates to a lack of market awareness, despite the tight tonnage list.
Several fixtures were reported, including Cepsa taking the Seabliss for a 130,000 mt Nigeria-to-Huelva voyage for a Nov. 11 laycan at w750.
Repsol also secured the Sonic for a 130,000 mt Nigeria-toSpain voyage with a Nov. 12 laycan, though the rate was not reported.
Clearlake was heard to have fixed the New Liberty for a Nov. 9 laycan, loading 130,000 mt in Cap Lopez for a UKCM discharge at w715, with an East discharge option at w745.
The same broker found the New Liberty fixture unusual, noting that while the rate was very low, it had the potential to negatively influence market sentiment.
Moeve also took the Thena for a 130,000 mt Nigeria-to-Spain voyage with a Nov. 11 laycan at a reported w745.
Based on the day's activity, Platts assessed the benchmark 130,000 mt WAF-UK/Continent route at w782.5, an increase of w47.5 day over day.
In the Black Sea, cargoes for the final 10 days of the month were being worked privately, while interest from the Mediterranean for early November dates was also on the rise, keeping sentiment firm.
Irving was reported to have taken the Front Seol for a 130,000 mt voyage from Sidi Kerir to Canaport for a Nov. 9 laycan, at a reported rate of w640.
Platts assessed freight on the 135,000 mt Black SeaMediterranean route at w1082.5, up w27.5 day over day.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Cresida Rodrigues

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 23, 2026

"If it doesn't, it will end up without enough crude to process," González said.
The emphasis on upstream development builds on Pemex's February 2025 work plan, which already contemplated substantial investment in exploration, drilling and field development.
However, the announcement has renewed questions about the company's ability to finance its ambitions, particularly given its continued dependence on government support.
Observers question the program's financial viability, arguing that government transfers, tax reductions and additional borrowing could shift the financial burden rather than resolve Pemex's underlying cash constraints.
"Reliance on funds guaranteed by the government, dependent on development banks like Banobras, represents a systemic risk, disguised as institutional financing," said Gabriel Becerra Dingler, an independent energy analyst and partner at Solmex Energía, a clean energy solutions company.
Betting on complementary private investments requires an environment of trust, rule of law and attractive returns, which the government has systematically eroded with its recent decisions, he said.
Pemex's recent reduction in debt has depended on federal assistance rather than internally generated cash surpluses, with free cash flow remaining negative after investment, observers say.
The Mexican Institute for Competitiveness estimates that Pemex received approximately Peso 1.88 trillion in federal support between January 2019 and June 2026, including capital contributions, tax incentives and extraordinary assistance.
The amount is roughly equivalent to the investment program now proposed, although the figures cover different periods and purposes.
Yet, those resources have done little to solve the situation the state oil and gas company faces. Pemex reported that its financial debt had declined 21% from the end of 2024 to $77.5 billion in June 2026, an improvement management said would reduce interest expenses and free resources for productive investment. The debt to its contractors remains elevated, estimated at roughly $20 billion by the first semester of 2026.
Workforce restructuring The investment program coincides with efforts to reduce
personnel costs and streamline Pemex's organizational structure. Mexican media have reported that dismissals and salary-
grade reductions are beginning to affect employees across several areas of the company, citing representatives of the National Union of Petroleum Technicians and Professionals.
The organization reported dismissals in Reynosa, Tamaulipas, and reductions in salary grades for some employees who remained with the company. The total number of affected workers has not been established.
The simultaneous pursuit of greater upstream investment and a smaller workforce raises questions about Pemex's ability to retain the technical and managerial expertise required for drilling and field development, according to observers.
-- Sheky Espejo

OIL FUTURES: ICE Brent highest since April on Middle East supply risksICE December Brent tops $116/b ...from page 1
Observed and assumed vessel movements through the Strait of Hormuz increased to 23 on Oct. 22, up from a revised 19 transits recorded on Oct. 21, CAS data showed, while transits through the Bab al-Mandab Strait declined to 27 from 35 a day earlier.
"The threat is no longer one route or one front: Iran, the Houthis in Yemen and militias in Iraq can all put Middle East oil infrastructure at risk," according to the latest crude oil market outlook from S&P Global CERA.
Saudi crude continues to reach international markets, including via Yanbu, the Suez Canal and Egypt's SUMED pipeline, but the latest attacks have put the market on edge over both production infrastructure and alternative export routes out of the Middle East.
"The rally is being driven less by current fundamentals than by mounting supply concerns," Patterson and Manthey said. "Even if tensions don't re-escalate, the mere possibility is likely to keep a sizeable geopolitical risk premium embedded in prices."
-- Christopher Vanmoessner
Refinery updates
REFINERY NEWS: India's BPCL throughput up 6% in Q2
Refinery: Three
Owner: Bharat Petroleum Corp Ltd.
Overall capacity: 35.3 million mt (706,000 b/d)
India's No.2 state-run refiner Bharat Petroleum Corp. Ltd. processed 10.41 million mt (826,000 b/d) in the July-September period, or the second quarter, company officials said on Oct. 23, reflecting 6% year-over-year rise in processing despite the Middle East conflict.
In Q2, BPCL refined throughput 2.6% higher than in the previous quarter (January-March), they said.
In Q2, BPCL sold 13.47 million mt of refined products, up 6.3% from a year ago and down 1.1% from the previous quarter, they said.
In Q2, BPCL exported 590,000 mt of refined products, up 63.9% a year ago and 15.7% from the January-March quarter, the company said in a regulatory filing.
BPCL operates three refineries: Mumbai on the west coast, Kochi in south India and Bina in central India, according to its website.
BPCL's fiscal year runs from April to March, the company said.
For the April-September period, or H1, BPCL processed 20.56 million mt, up 1.6% year over year, they said.
In H1, BPCL's sales stood at 27 million mt, up 3.2% year over year, they said.

REFINERY NEWS: Eni's Italy throughput rises 9% in Q3
Refineries and biorefineries in Italy: Gela, Porto Marghera, Milazzo, Sannazzaro, Taranto, Livorno
Jointly owned refineries abroad: Bayernoil and Schwedt (Germany), Ruwais (UAE)
Owner: Eni

Italy's Augusta refinery is up and running but cannot load fuel trucks because of nationwide protests, the company told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, on Oct. 23.
Protesters against high fuel prices and rising living costs have blocked access to the refinery, local media reported Oct. 22.
The fuel trucks are blocked from reaching oil depots, the reports said.
Last week, another Italian refinery Taranto was blocked by a strike by workers from the Ilva steelworks, though the blockades were lifted and loadings resumed after a week.
-- Elza Turner
Subscriber Notes

Throughput at Eni S.p.A.'s Italian refineries rose 9% year over year to 4.14 million metric tons in the third quarter of 2026, the company said Oct. 23.
In the first nine months of 2026, throughput was down 5% at 10.28 million mt.
The Q3 increase was attributed to "higher volumes at the Taranto refinery due to higher plant availability."
Eni's Q3 throughput in other refineries globally was down 12% to 2.47 million mt "due to lower volumes processed connected to the closure of the Strait of Hormuz." Throughput was also 19% down at 6.43 million mt in January-September.
The company reported an average utilization rate of 86% across its refineries in Q3, up from 84% in Q3 2025 and 76% in the nine months of 2026, down from 81% in the same year-ago period.
Eni's benchmark refining margin in Q3 was $28.1/b, up from $8.9/b a year earlier, "mainly due to more favorable middle distillate crack spreads reflecting the continuing product tightness in connection with disrupted flows from the Middle East and plant outages against a backdrop of refinery closures in the Atlantic Basin."
In January-September, the margin was $15.5/b, up from $5.8/b. Bio throughput in Q3 was 262,000 mt, down from 315,000 mt in the year-ago quarter, "driven by lower volumes processed at the Gela and Venice biorefineries following the planned shutdown, in order to enhance biorefinery configuration, partially offset by better performance of Chalmette biorefinery." In January-September, bio throughput was 789,000 mt, down from 881,000 mt, "mainly reflecting the shutdown at Venice." Average utilization was 71% in Q3, down from 85% year over year, and 70% in January-September, down from 79% in the same year-ago period.
-- Elza Turner

Platts to launch forward derivative assessments for STS Lome gasoline, diesel and jet fuel
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has noted increasing demand for transparency on West African refined products, both on a spot market basis and for forward delivery. In response, Platts will start publishing daily forward swap assessments for the existing Gasoline STS Lome, Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome, and Jet Fuel STS Lome physical price assessments, effective Nov. 30, 2026. These will be published as Month 1, Month 2, and Month 3 assessments.
In order to assess the derivatives on an editorial basis, Platts will consult a range of market information, including information heard in the physical market, relevant forward exchange-traded data, as well as related markets.
The published assessments will correspond with delivery dates three months forward, starting with the front month [M1], followed by M2 and M3.

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).
The affected assessments appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Oilgram Price Report, LNG Daily, Platts OPR World Crude Table, Platts Global Alert/Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 1250, 1805, 2202, 2203, 2211, 2216, 2217, 2244, 2245, 2264, 2265, 2280, 2281, 2811 and 2812, LNG Alert pages 0050 and 0051, Platts Natural Gas Alert pages 1024 and 1069, and under the price database codes below:
