===== OILGRAM PRICE REPORT dated 2026-10-26 (crude-relevant pages only) =====
Platts Oilgram Price Report
Volume 104 / Issue 205 / October 26, 2026

News

Market analysis

OIL FUTURES: Crude pulls back from recent highs despite continued Middle East tensions
Six VLCCs exit Strait of Hormuz
Iran launches 29 attacks on ships this month: IMO
1.5 billion crude barrels exited Hormuz since summer
Crude oil futures settled lower Oct. 26 as traders booked profits against a backdrop of continued Strait of Hormuz vessel flows and persistent Middle East escalation risks.
NYMEX December WTI settled $1.51/barrel lower at $101.76/b and ICE December Brent declined 91 cents/b to $115.81/b.
"The pullback comes despite continued Houthi attacks on Saudi Arabian infrastructure over the weekend, suggesting some technical profit-taking after last week's sharp upswing," Priyanka Sachdeva, Head of Market Insights from Phillip Nova, said in a note Oct. 26. Neither Aramco nor the Saudi government acknowledged the attacks.
Strait of Hormuz vessel transits were steady at 17 on Oct. 25 and included six VLCC movements, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 26 report, despite continued attacks on shipping.
Iran has launched its heaviest wave of attacks on merchant ships around Hormuz and in the wider region this month, with the International Maritime Organization recording 29 attacks during Oct. 1-22, exceeding all monthly tallies since March.
"The threat is no longer one route or one front: Iran, the Houthis in Yemen and militias in Iraq can all put Middle East oil infrastructure at risk," according to the latest crude oil market outlook from S&P Global Energy CERA.
(continued on page 39)

International Crude
ASIA CRUDE OIL: Key market indicators for Oct. 26-30
Middle East complex eyes fresh producer sell tenders issuance
Regional complex to see movement of remaining Dec-loading barrels
Crude oil prices are expected to remain under pressure in the week ending Oct. 30 as traders monitor US-Iran diplomatic progress and steady oil flows from the Middle East.
The front-month December ICE Brent crude futures contract traded at $114.58/b at 10:37 am Singapore time (0237 GMT) Oct. 26, down $2.14/b, or 1.83%, from the previous settlement.
Middle East crude ** Middle East crude traders will be watching for the issuance
of any fresh producer sell tenders over the week ending Oct. 30, while spot trade in Iraqi Basrah crudes as well as Far East Russia's ESPO Blend crude is also expected to continue.
** The front-month Dubai cash-futures spread averaged at a premium of $8.67/b over Oct. 1-23, down $12.93/b from a $21.60/b premium over September, Platts data showed.
Asia-Pacific regional crude ** The Asia-Pacific regional complex is anticipated to see spot
trading activity dwindling over Oct. 26-30 across the crude and condensate barrels as the current December-loading trade cycle inches to a close.
** The complex is also expected to see regional refiners sourcing January-arrival barrels of WAF crude as a source of alternative crude grades.
** Markets are also keeping an eye on the emergence of Brunei's September OSP, likely in the week.

Delivered crude ** Trading activity for February-arrival barrels of US WTI Midland
crude is expected to commence in the week ending Oct. 30, with the crude grade scheduled for January-arrival last heard traded at a premium in the mid $20s/b against Dated Brent, CFR Taiwan.
** Market activity for January-arrival barrels of Brazilian Tupi crude is anticipated to continue, with the crude grade last heard traded at a premium of around $10/b against Dated Brent, DES China.
(continued on page 3)

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

Oil futures ** Crude oil prices are expected to remain rangebound over
Oct. 26-30 as traders monitor US-Iran diplomatic progress and steady oil flows from the Middle East.
** Crude oil futures retreated from near six-month highs during the Oct. 26 Asian mid-morning session, on profit-taking as investors keep track of fresh developments between US and Iran after US President Donald Trump indicated he was nearing a decision on Iran.
** The front-month ICE contract declined 2.05% from the previous settlement to $114.33/b at 11 am SGT on Oct. 26, while the front-month NYMEX WTI light sweet crude contract declined 2.25% to $100.95/b at 11 am SGT.
** Platts assessed the prompt M1-M2 ICE Brent crude time spread at $4.88/b at the Oct. 26 11 am SGT, up from $4.61/b on Oct. 23 Asian close.
-- Leon Wong, Yong ren Toh, Yue Wang
Oman's OQEP H1 output rises 3% with global deal push
OQEP output rises to 228,200 boe/d by June 30
Block acquisition to add 3,500 boe/d in 2026
Partnerships span Kazakhstan, Libya
Oman's OQ Exploration and Production reported production of 228,200 barrels of oil equivalent by the end of the first half of 2026, up 2.7% from a year earlier, with a target of 300,000 boe/d by 2030 as the company focuses on building domestic properties while making international acquisitions, Oman News Agency reported Oct. 25, citing a statement from the company.
"The strategy driving this trend combines organic growth in Oman with targeted acquisitions and selective international expansion," according to the statement.
OEQP has brought in partners to develop its production within Oman, including plans announced in June to develop Block 80 for oil and gas resources, in partnership with Turkish Petroleum Overseas Company Limited.
It's also signed an agreement to acquire a 35% non-operated participating interest in Block 27 from Mitsui E&P Middle East; the block is operated by Occidental, and the interest is expected to add about 3,500 boe/d to OQEP's net production in 2026, OQEP said in the statement.
OQEP also signed a concession agreement for offshore Block 18 in partnership with Petronas, covering 21,000 square kilometers. OQEP already also works with BP, Shell, TotalEnergies and Eni, according to the statement.
Oman's crude production reached 940,000 barrels/day in September, according to the latest Platts OPEC+ survey by S&P Global Energy. The country has been working to attract foreign investment into its energy sector under its Vision 2040 program to enhance recovery from mature fields while developing new resources.
Internationally, OQEP is working with KazMunayGas to explore potential upstream investments in Kazakhstan and Oman, it said.

OQEP also signed a memorandum of understanding with the Libyan Investment Authority to explore joint upstream investment opportunities in Oman, Libya and other international markets, according to the statement.
-- Claudia Carpenter
CRUDE MOC: Middle East sour crude complex sees four convergences
98 Dubai partials trade during MOC
Dec-loading Oman, Murban cargoes declared

The Middle East sour crude complex saw four convergences during the Singapore Platts Market on Close assessment process Oct. 26, while cash differentials for key sour crude markers slid for a second day.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed December cash Dubai, Oman and Murban all at a premium of $6.69/b to samemonth Dubai futures at the market close, down $1.30/b on the day.
During the MOC, 98 December Dubai partials of 25,000 barrels each traded. The sellers were Mitsui, BP, Mercuria, Unipec, Vitol, Glencore and Gunvor, while the buyers were Trafigura, BP, ExxonMobil and Phillips 66.
Vitol, Mitsui and Unipec each declared a cargo of December Oman crude and BP a cargo of December Murban crude to Trafigura following convergences of 20 partials in Platts cash Dubai.
A convergence occurs when 20 partials are traded between two counterparties in the same direction, resulting in a full 500,000-barrel physical cargo being declared from the seller to the buyer.

Singapore to store Saudi crude at Jurong Rock Caverns from H2 2027
Aramco, Singapore authorities exchange MOU Negotiations to conclude in a few months

Singapore and Saudi Arabia have announced their joint intent to establish an arrangement for Saudi crude oil to be stored at the Jurong Rock Caverns from the second half of 2027, Singapore's Minister for Energy, Trade and Industry Tan See Leng said at the Singapore International Energy Week on Oct. 26.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

3

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.



Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

"This arrangement enhances the resilience of the Saudi crude oil supply chain in Southeast Asia, as well as contributes to Singapore's energy security and resilience in the event of supply disruptions," Tan said.
Singapore's Energy Market Authority and Jurong Town Corp. exchanged a memorandum of understanding with Saudi Aramco at SIEW on the day as a first step toward establishing the arrangement, according to a release from the Ministry of Energy, Trade and Industry. Tan said in his speech that the parties aim to conclude negotiations within a few months.
"Singapore's Ministry of Energy, Trade and Industry and the Kingdom of Saudi Arabia's Ministry of Energy will work closely to advance the arrangement and coordinate its implementation with relevant entities," according to the release.
A METI spokesperson told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, on Oct. 26, "As these arrangements are commercially sensitive, we are unable to disclose further details."
Saudi Aramco declined to comment on Oct. 26. Jurong Rock Caverns, comprising four caverns with 330,000 cubic meters each and one with 150,000 cu m, can store up to 1.47 million cu m, equivalent to about 9.25 million barrels of liquid hydrocarbons such as crude oil and condensate, according to information on JTC's and Vopak's websites. Aramco's current overseas storage and delivery network includes hubs in Rotterdam, Egypt's Sidi Kerir, Ulsan in South Korea, and the Japanese terminals of Okinawa and Kiire, according to the company's website. Earlier in October, Aramco CEO Amin Nasser told the WPC

Energy Congress in Riyadh that global commercial onshore oil inventories of less than 6 billion barrels offered "barely a cushion at all" against unforeseen disruptions, adding that replenishing inventories while meeting demand could take up to two years.
-- Mia Pei, Sambit Mohanty
Norway's BW Energy takes Gabon oil asset offline after rig sinks
Borr's Prospector 5 sinks in Dussafu permit
Five workers missing, 110 accounted for
Some 26,000 b/d output temporarily offline
Some 26,000 barrels/day of crude output from BW Energy's Dussafu Marin license off Gabon is temporarily offline after a drilling rig sank over the weekend, leaving as many as five people dead.
In a statement released on its website on Oct. 25, the Norwegian company said an incident occurred on the Prospector 5 rig, owned by Borr Drilling, at 2037 local time on Oct. 24.
"During jacking operations in preparation for a planned drilling campaign, the rig experienced a loss of stability and subsequently sank," the company said. Of the 115 workers onboard, 11 have been accounted for and five are missing.
A search and rescue mission is underway involving BW, rig owner Borr Drilling, and the emergency services, it said.
The company added that production at the MaBoMo facility is "shut down in accordance with standard procedure during

"There has been no reported damage to the facility or related equipment, nor any accidental discharge of harmful substances to the sea from the facility," it said.
The Dussafu permit pumped 33,200 b/d of crude in 2025, according to the company, with a slew of subsea wells tied back to the MaBoMo platform. Crude is then piped 20 kilometers to the Adolo FPSO for export.

The company has already started work on its MaBoMo Phase 2 project, which should add 20,000 b/d of production from four new producing wells on the key license and see first oil in Q1 2027, according to BW's Q2 earnings presentation.
Looking further ahead, another two appraisal wells on Dussafu Marin could lead to another 10,000 b/d of output, according to BW.

Platts Asia Pacific Sweet Crude Daily Market Analysis
Regional complex to see remaining Dec-loading barrels moved
US raises African oil and gas spending on fossil fuel pivot
The Asia-Pacific regional complex is anticipated to see spot trading activity dwindle over Oct. 26-30 across crude and condensate barrels as the current December-loading trade cycle inches to a close.
Markets are also keeping an eye on the emergence of Brunei's September OSP, likely in the week.
In the arbitrage crude complex, valuations of Januaryarrival barrels of Canadian Access Western Blend were heard at a premium in the $2s/b to $3s/b against March ICE Brent, DES China.
Trading activity for February-arrival barrels of US WTI Midland crude is also expected to commence in the week ending Oct. 30, with the crude grade scheduled for January arrival last heard

traded at a premium in the mid-$20s/b against Dated Brent, CFR Taiwan.
Meanwhile, the US is taking on a growing role in funding African oil and gas projects as it has shifted its support for green energy and rejected forecasts of fossil fuel demand reaching a plateau, according to government officials, analysts and private investors.
A lack of Western funding from climate-conscious lenders is often cited by Africa's oil and gas advocates as producing an unfair disadvantage for the continent, where many countries lack the financing to develop their natural resources.
According to analysis from S&P Global Energy CERA, capital expenditure on African exploration and production peaked at $57 billion in 2013 before retreating to $20 billion in 2021, largely due to IOCs, including Chevron and ExxonMobil, slashing spending.
Elsewhere, the reduction of gas supplies from Indonesia is less than 2% of Singapore's total daily gas demand, said Singapore's Minister for Energy, Trade and Industry Tan See Leng at Singapore International Energy Week on Oct. 26.
Addressing Indonesia's announcement over the weekend that gas suppliers have reduced their piped gas exports to Singapore, Tan noted that Singapore can "keep calm and carry on."
On Oct. 25, SKK Migas chief Djoko Siswanto told Platts that Indonesia had reduced natural gas exports to Singapore by a combined 31 million standard cubic feet/day from Oct. 24, redirecting the gas to meet its domestic power demand.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Leon Wong
Platts Middle East Sour Crude Daily Market Analysis
Indian Oil buys Basrah Medium, Heavy crude for Dec. delivery
Platts Dubai differential down for second day as demand eases
Spot trading in Iraqi Basrah crude continued in the Middle East sour crude market Oct. 26, while demand across Asia softened as the trading cycle approached month-end.
Indian Oil Corp. Ltd., in a recently concluded tender, purchased 2 million barrels of Basrah Medium crude at a discount of about $2.25/barrel to Platts November Dubai crude assessments on a DAP basis, as well as 1 million barrels of Basrah Heavy crude at a discount of about $7.20/b to the same marker. The cargoes are expected to arrive in Paradip and Mundra in mid-December.
More details also emerged on a Khafji crude sale in the previous week ended Oct. 23, with traders saying 2 million barrels each were likely awarded to four end users at premiums of about $8/b to Platts December Dubai crude assessments on a delivered basis.
In the broader market, differentials for Middle East crude markers weakened Oct. 26, with most Asian refiners likely having concluded their purchases for the month and traders looking to clear remaining unsold barrels.
Benchmark Platts cash Dubai's differential against samemonth Dubai futures slipped for a second day, falling $1.30/b to a $6.69/b premium at the Oct. 26 Asian close.

Platts Mediterranean & Black Sea Sweet Crude Daily Commentary
CPC Blend differentials sink further
High freight costs block arb opportunities
Ample cargo availability in the Mediterranean sweet crude market continued to weigh on prices in the region Oct. 26.
The elevated freight environment has limited the arbitrage opportunities for unplaced barrels to move outside the region, with traders continuing to note that a further decline in FOB differentials is required to offset the transportation costs.
"It feels a fairly rough market. Things [are] dropping off a cliff for November compared to recent months. Everything apart from freight," said one Med crude trader Oct. 26.
CPC Blend cargoes loading over the third-decade of November were heard offered at a discount of $8/b to Dated Brent in early European trade, while second-decade November loaders were heard pegged at a discount of $9/b to Dated Brent.
The values reflected a further weakening of differentials from the previous week.
"[There's] too much CPC Blend and [it] needs to clear the Med," the trader said, noting that the reopening of arbitrage incentives for the Black Sea grade remained "dollars away."
During the Platts Market on Close assessment process, Petroineos and Chevron each offered a CPC Blend cargo loading over third-decade November dates, with both offers demonstrating weaker values on the day.
ExxonMobil lifted Chevron's offer for a cargo loading over Nov. 23-27 at a discount of $8.90/b to Dated Brent, while Petroineos withdrew its Nov. 20-24 loading offer at a discount of $8.85/b to Dated Brent.
Little activity was heard in the Azeri Light market during the session. The grade had already retreated significantly due to sellside pressure earlier in the month.
Meanwhile, November volumes of Basrah Medium were heard to be largely sold.
Barrels of the Iraqi crude, along with other Middle Eastern flows, have been a key source of pressure on the European sour segment in recent weeks.
The Strait of Hormuz had six VLCC transits on Oct. 25, the most since Oct. 17, while the Bab al-Mandab Strait recorded one VLCC on both Oct. 24 and Oct. 25, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 26 report.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Ernest Puey

Platts North Sea Crude Daily Market Analysis
Volatile freight economics keep trading activity muted
Looser supply balance expected toward end of Nov
Trading activity across the North Sea crude complex remained sluggish in line with underlying volatility Oct. 26, although expectations had clustered around looser supply balances going forward.
There has been "no change [in the market]," said a Europebased North Sea crude trader. "There are still some availabilities I would think, the market is slow for WTI Midland."
Thin trading in both the over-the-counter market and the Platts Market on Close assessment process for WTI Midland had coincided with trader reports that Asian buying for the grade had been seen in recent sessions.
One notable facet of the activity was that a sizable proportion of recent US Gulf Coast-Far East flows had been fixed on an Aframax basis, said traders, an unusual inversion of typical freight economics which favor larger vessels for long-haul journeys.
While traders continued to note volatility across landed WTI Midland values into Europe in line with punitive freight economics, market participants largely saw looser supply balances going forward with barrels from West Africa, the Mediterranean and Latin America pointing toward Europe.
"Markets are all over the place, it's hard to tie down value," said a second trader, adding nonetheless that "there are still North Sea barrels to be placed for second-half November."
In line with an anticipated weakening of values going forward, traders continued to peg values for mid-November loading Forties cargoes as low as $3/b premiums over Dated Brent during Oct. 26 European trading.
Bearish sentiment was also reflected across the sour segment, with traders expecting the downward pressure on Norway's Johan Sverdrup seen across the previous week to continue going forward.
Availabilities for the grade were ample, with the Novemberloading program "far from done," said a third trader.
The Oct. 26 MOC session saw BP offering a Johan Sverdrup FOB Mongstad cargo loading across Nov. 5-7, left outstanding at the London close without finding a buyer at a $1.15/b premium over Dated Brent.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Natasha Tan

Americas Crude
US crude oil stocks expected lower amid strong refinery demand
Inventories expected to hit four-week low
Refinery runs likely edge up to 93.6% of capacity
Exports decline as arbitrage economics weaken
US crude oil inventory draws likely extended during the week to Oct. 23, analysts surveyed by Platts, a part of S&P Global Energy, said Oct. 26, against a backdrop of stronger refinery demand.
Commercial crude stocks likely declined 1.2 million barrels to around 411.3 million barrels during the period, analysts said. This fourth straight weekly draw would put stocks at the lowest level since late July and leave them 3.6% behind the seasonal five-year average of US Energy Information Administration data.
Nationwide refinery utilization likely climbed 0.2 percentage point to 93.6% of capacity, analysts said, a five-week high and 6.8% above average for this time of year.
Refinery margins have broadly declined during October but remain robust, S&P Global CERA data showed. The US Gulf Coast WTI MEH cracking margin has averaged $48.72/barrel to-date this month, down from $50.13/b during September.
Distillate cracks remained very strong. The USGC ULSD crack versus WTI MEH has averaged $100.13/b in October compared with $101.03/b in September, CERA data showed.
US distillate stocks likely climbed 600,000 barrels to around 104.9 million barrels during the week to Oct. 23, analysts said,

while gasoline stocks were seen up 500,000 barrels over the period at around 205.8 million barrels.
Weekly US crude exports slowed to 3.19 million b/d, according to data from S&P Global Commodities at Sea, falling to the lowest level since early August.
Export arbitrage economics remained supportive along key routes, but have weakened in recent weeks. The incentive for moving WTI MEH into Rotterdam versus North Sea Forties has averaged $14.36/b to date in October, CERA data showed, but averaged just $6.83/b during the week to Oct. 23.
The incentive for exporting Mars crude into China versus Dubai fell to minus $1.57/b during the reporting period, compared with an October-to-date average of $4.76/b, CERA data showed.
-- Christopher Vanmoessner
Ovintiv acquires Canadian light oil producer Logan Energy for C$1.26 billion
Montney assets produce 20,000 boe/d
Deal includes 24,000 b/d crude storage
Second major deal after NuVista Energy
Ovintiv Inc. unveiled a definitive agreement to buy Calgarybased light oil producer Logan Energy Corp. under a C$1.26 billion ($890 million) deal, further extending its footprint in the Western Canadian Sedimentary Basin's shale resources, it said Oct. 26.
The acquisition will add nearly 20,000 barrels/day of oil equivalent production, including 7,000 b/d of crude oil and condensate, 70 million cubic feet/day of natural gas, and over 1 million b/d of other NGLs such as C2 (ethane) and C4 (butane), Ovintiv said in a release.
The deal also provides the company access to 420 net 10,000-foot-equivalent oil well locations (including 220 premium well locations at an average cost of $1.5 million per premium location) and about 266,000 net acres of land position in the Montney oil window, it said, noting some 80% of those acres are undeveloped.
Logan's assets are located in the Simonette and Pouce Coupe areas, which are in close proximity to Ovintiv's existing Montney operations and also include two new oil batteries and additional natural gas processing capacity, it said.
With the deal, Ovintiv's core land position in the Montney is expected to increase to nearly 720,000 net acres, the release said, adding that 60,000 net acres with exposure to the Duvernay shale play are also included in the transaction.
Logan has 100% ownership in two new crude oil batteries (storage facilities) in Simonette, with a total capacity of 24,000 b/d, the first of which came online during the third quarter of 2026, and the second is expected to come online by the end of the first quarter of 2027, the Ovintiv release said.
Logan also has 50% ownership at a natural gas processing facility in Simonette with a total capacity of 90 million cubic feet/ day and 65% ownership in a natural gas processing facility in Pouce Coupe with a total capacity of 57 MMcf/d, it said.

Outright prices for crude on the USGC weakened Oct. 26 as markets reacted to continued vessel flows through the Strait of Hormuz and persistent escalation risks in the Middle East.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed WTI Midland unchanged at a 60-cent/barrel premium to WTI. On an outright basis, WTI Midland fell $7.01 to $102.36/b.
On a volume-weighted basis, Platts assessed WTI Midland at a 63 cent/b premium to WTI, down 55 cents day over day. This was based on a volume of 248,00 barrels.
Platts assessed WTI Houston at a $1.15/b premium to WTI, down 5 cents day over day. On an outright basis, WTI Houston finished down $6.81 at $102.91/b.
Month-decade premiums to WTI at the Magellan East Houston terminal were unchanged, with the premium for first-decade loaders assessed at MEH plus $1.50/b, second-decade loaders at MEH plus 75 cents/b, and third-decade loaders at MEH minus 10 cents/b.
Iran has launched its heaviest wave of attacks on merchant ships around the Strait of Hormuz and in the wider region this month, with the International Maritime Organization recording 29 attacks during Oct. 1-22, exceeding all monthly tallies since March.
Strait of Hormuz vessel transits were steady at 17 on Oct. 25 and included six VLCC movements, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 26 report, despite continued attacks on shipping.
US forces marked the export of at least 1.5 billion barrels of crude oil since the summer through the Strait of Hormuz, with US Central Command commander Admiral Brad Cooper flying over the chokepoint in a US Army CH-47 Chinook, CENTCOM said on X Oct. 25. The post noted that more than 3,000 commercial ships had successfully transited the Strait of Hormuz with support from CENTCOM forces over the last six months.

Gasoline
Europe gasoline-naphtha spreads enter contango as blending weakens
Nov-Dec spread moves to 25 cents/mt contango
Blending economics erode for European suppliers
Cash differentials incentivize destocking amid low stocks: sources
European gasoline-naphtha spreads moved into contango Oct. 23 as weakening gasoline margins and stronger naphtha values continued to erode blending economics for European gasoline suppliers.

The November 2026 gasoline-naphtha spread fell $8.25/ metric ton to $134.75/mt on Oct. 23, putting it 25 cents/mt below the December 2026 spread, according to pricing data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
Further along the curve, the December 2026/January 2027 spread fell to a $3.50/mt discount on Oct. 23, widening its contango by $1.75/mt day over day.
European gasoline-naphtha spreads have sold off sharply through October, with naphtha supported by strong propane demand from the petrochemical sector while gasoline enters a seasonal demand lull. At the same time, European refiners have faced increasing difficulty placing gasoline barrels overseas, limiting export demand as clean tanker freight rates have surged amid tight vessel availability.
During European midmorning trading Oct. 26, the contango structure became more established amid increased liquidity.
Alongside the erosion in naphtha's blending value for European gasoline suppliers, gasoline cracks have also come under considerable pressure. The front-month FOB AmsterdamRotterdam-Antwerp Eurobob crack fell 63 cents/barrel to $17.87/ barrel on Oct. 23, down $6.18/b on the week and $16.15/b since the start of October.
The M2 crack declined more moderately, falling $2.34/b week over week to $17.16/b on Oct. 23.
Gasoline margins have come under particular pressure while cracks for other European refined products have remained comparatively strong. Platts assessed the FOB ARA ULSD 10 ppmS crack at $87.41/b on Oct. 23, down just 27 cents/b since the start of October. The FOB Rotterdam jet fuel crack stood at $90.69/b on Oct. 23, up $1.65/b over the same period, Platts assessed.
Looking ahead, market participants expect further destocking as prompt cash differentials remain strong. The cash/November differential for FOB ARA Eurobob was assessed at $53.50/mt on Oct. 23, while the cash/December differential was substantially stronger at $95.50/mt.
Attention is now turning to whether stronger demand through November can support outright gasoline values and improve blending economics, particularly with European gasoline inventories already at multiyear lows.
"Cash diffs should sell off more," a European gasoline trader said.
Gasoline stocks in the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp hub fell 14.41% to 707,000 metric tons in the week ended Oct. 22, their lowest level since Sept. 2, 2021.
Length building may, however, conflict with heightened concerns over tightening supply in the Mediterranean, with traders angling to draw needed volumes into southern markets.
"Mediterranean is getting tighter indeed, [and we] need to pull product from the ARA," a second European trader said.
The November Mediterranean/North gasoline differential, representing the spread between the November FOB Mediterranean swap and the equivalent FOB ARA Eurobob barge, was unchanged at a $21/mt premium. The December differential was also unchanged, at a $22/mt premium.
-- Friedrich Gutsche

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Jet Daily Market Analysis
Cash differential turns negative on supply glut; at 1-month low
Saudi crude to be stored at Singapore's Jurong Rock caverns
ARA jet fuel stocks fall 6.13% week over week to 461,000 mt
Bearish demand-supply fundamentals kept a lid on the Asian jet fuel/kerosene complex Oct. 26, reflected by a narrowing market structure and cash differential.
Platts assessed the FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene cargo cash differential at minus 18 cents/barrel to the Mean of Platts Singapore jet fuel/kerosene assessments at the Oct. 23 Asian

close, slipping 30 cents/b day over day from plus 12 cents/b the previous session.
During the Platts Singapore physical jet fuel/kerosene Market on Close process Oct. 23, Unipec bought a Nov. 7-11 loading cargo from Vitol at MOPS minus 48 cents/b after normalization.
The cash differential was last lower at minus $1.28/b Sept. 23, according to Platts data, marking its weakest value in a month.
The shift into negative territory coincided with a fresh wave of offers for November-loading spot cargoes from South Korea, adding to a glut that has weighed on the market since China resumed refined product exports following a brief suspension during the Golden Week holidays.
Record freight rates have further weighed on demand, compounding already limited regional shorts, sources said.
"The overhang from October is huge, plus freight is killing everything," said a Singapore-based jet fuel trader.
"Buying interest is very thin at the moment, we don't see any big buy tenders for jet, and freight is very expensive. Moving cargoes is still expensive despite lower FOB spot prices," said a second participant based in Singapore, adding that arbitrage opportunities have also been dampened.
GS Caltex canceled its tender offering 300,000 or 450,000 barrels of jet A-1 fuel for loading Nov. 8-12 from Yeosu. The tender was due to close Oct. 23, with same-day validity.
The exact reason for the cancellation could not be confirmed, but some participants attributed it to potentially low bids. GS Caltex did not respond to a request for official comment from Platts Oct. 23.
In the derivatives segment, brokers pegged the front-month November-December FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene swap time spread -- an indicator of the near-term market outlook -- at plus $1.42/b in midmorning Asia trading Oct. 26, narrowing from the Platts assessment of plus $1.51/b at the Oct. 23 Asian close.
In other news, Singapore and Saudi Arabia will soon finalize an arrangement for Saudi crude oil to be stored at the Jurong Rock caverns from the second half of 2027, Singapore's Minister for Energy, Trade and Industry Tan See Leng told the Singapore International Energy Week Oct. 26.
"This arrangement enhances the resilience of the Saudi crude oil supply chain in Southeast Asia, as well as contributes to Singapore's energy security and resilience in the event of supply disruptions," Tan said.
He added that Saudi Aramco will sign an MOU with the Energy Market Authority and Jurong Town Corporation later in the day. The parties aim to conclude negotiations in a few months.
Elsewhere, jet fuel stocks in the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp refining hub declined 6.13% week over week to 461,000 metric tons in the week to Oct. 22, as seasonal demand for jet fuel eased and supply was tight, according to Oct. 22 data from Insights Global.
Inventories were nearly half the levels seen in the same week of 2025, when stocks stood at about 1.17 million metric tons.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Shu ling Lee

The lawmakers argued that the license does not resolve statutory restrictions on Russian energy imports. Their letter cited the Ending Importation of Russian Oil Act of 2022, which they said generally prohibits such imports unless the president consults Congress and certifies that Russia has ceased military hostilities in Ukraine, poses no immediate military threat to NATO members and recognizes Ukrainians' right to choose their government.
They also cited Section 216 of the Countering America's Adversaries Through Sanctions Act, arguing that the administration had not fulfilled congressional review requirements for licensing actions that significantly alter US foreign policy toward Russia.
The warning extended beyond importers of record. Companies knowingly buying Russian diesel or facilitating its importation could also face criminal penalties, the lawmakers said, urging Goldman Sachs not to support purchases, transportation, unloading, storage, financing or distribution until statutory requirements were satisfied.
The preservation demand covers contracts, shipping and customs documents, payment records, terminal reservations and communications with counterparties and US government officials beginning Sept. 1. The letter does not establish that Goldman Sachs has participated in a transaction.
Russian diesel heads to New York The congressional intervention comes as Russia prepares to
resume diesel shipments to the US. Russia Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak said a first tanker was scheduled to depart Russia's Baltic coast Oct. 22, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, reported Oct. 21. He previously outlined plans to supply 300,000 mt to the US in October and another 500,000 mt in November.
The Malta-flagged medium-sized tanker Agioi Fanendes is heading to New York carrying Russian diesel, according to S&P Global Energy Commodities at Sea data, with the Kremlin expecting deliveries to continue "on a regular basis," Kremlin spokesperson Dimitry Peskov said Oct. 23.
Russia had shipped about 400,000 mt of diesel internationally in October as of the Oct. 21 report, mostly to Egypt, Morocco, Turkey and Brazil, according to CAS data.
Trump's Oct. 9 announcement outlined 4.8 million mt of additional diesel supplies in stages, with the final 3 million mt dependent on the condition of Russian refineries. Russia initially confirmed its willingness to supply petroleum products but did not corroborate the full volume or delivery schedule.
The delivery of these diesel volumes to the US "would only be a displacement of volume from Brazil," a European diesel trader told Platts Oct. 23. "It is a logistical change regarding where the product goes, rather than an increase in flows. We would have to see vastly more flows for it to [affect] the European market."
Supply constraints persist The announcement initially pressured diesel margins. The
NYMEX front-month ULSD crack against WTI fell $6.45/b to

$96.80/b at 1933 GMT Oct. 9, although prices subsequently remained elevated. ICE low sulfur gasoil futures settled at $1,449.50/mt Oct. 20, compared with $1,420.75/mt Oct. 9, according to Platts assessments.
Russia's ability to restore exports remains constrained by refinery damage and domestic supply requirements. A Sept. 30 S&P Global Energy CERA outlook estimated that Ukrainian drone attacks had pushed 3.1 million b/d of Russian refining capacity offline, with third-quarter diesel loadings averaging 140,000 b/d, down 500,000 b/d year over year.
Novak said Russia could restore full diesel export volumes by year-end. CERA's September outlook was more cautious, forecasting that continuing attacks and domestic demand would prevent a return to pre-crisis exports by year-end and keep supplies constrained through 2027.

Switzerland authorizes temporary use of diesel stocks
Pipeline delays trigger release
45,000 cubic meters available through Nov. 22
Switzerland has authorized the temporary release of diesel stocks to address short-term shortages due to recent pipeline delivery delays, the Swiss Federal Office for National Economic Supply (FONES) said late Oct. 23.
The delays in pipeline deliveries were due to heavy rainfall in southern France. Supplies had already been "severely disrupted for months" due to low Rhine water levels.
"Drawing on mandatory reserves will help offset this temporary shortfall, ensuring that Switzerland's diesel supply remains secure," FONES said in the statement. Up to 45,000 cubic meters can be drawn between Oct. 23 and Nov. 22, FONES said. The volume corresponds to just under 4% of the available obligatory diesel stocks.
On Oct. 15, Switzerland also temporarily authorized the release of jet fuel stocks until Nov. 1 to maintain supplies at Zurich Airport.
FONES also said that supplies of gasoline and heating oil are currently guaranteed, although "the situation remains tight and precarious across all petroleum products."
-- Elza Turner

"Exports in November are set to fall as the domestic market is so tight," said a source with a state-run refinery in southern China, adding that Chinese refineries are required to prioritize meeting domestic supplies rather than capturing export profits.

Meanwhile, five of the six refiners who spoke to Platts said they would cut their utilization in November, capping production, as they expected to make losses due to high freight and insurance costs.

He added that the refinery plans to cut utilization to about 80% in November from about 93% in October.
Under China's current oil product pricing mechanism, introduced in 2013, the government adjusts retail ceiling prices for gasoline and gasoil every 10 working days based on a basket of benchmark crude prices, reportedly including ICE Brent, NYMEX WTI and DME Oman, weighted according to China's crude import profile.
Market sources said freight and insurance were set at a combined $5/b in the formula, much lower than the current level.

Platts European Diesel Daily Market Analysis
ICE gasoil futures climb $29.50/mt

The European diesel market remains elevated Oct. 26, with bullish underlying gasoil futures amid persistent risks of escalation in the Middle East.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month ICE low sulfur gasoil futures up $29.50/metric ton to $1,521.75/mt Oct. 26 as the market fears further disruption to oil operations in the Middle East.
"The threat is no longer one route or one front: Iran, the Houthis in Yemen and militias in Iraq can all put Middle East oil infrastructure at risk," according to the latest crude oil market outlook from S&P Global Energy CERA.
On diesel specifically, "people are worried about the sustainability of flows from the Persian Gulf and passing through the Strait of Hormuz," a Europe-based diesel trader said.
So far in October, 218,000 metric tons arrived in NWE, and 154,000 mt in the Med, S&P Global Commodities at Sea data showed. This is up significantly from a wartime low of just 69,000 mt in both regions combined in April, but remains suppressed relative to the 1.108 million mt total in October 2025.
"As you can see from the flat price, no one is comfortable," the diesel trader added.
Platts assessed the 10ppm diesel CIF NWE up $28.25/mt to $1,553/mt Oct. 26, the highest level since Sept. 24.
High flat prices are also being supported by elevated freight rates, according to market participants.
"It's an issue of availability of tonnage," a second Europebased diesel trader.
In other news, Switzerland has authorized the temporary release of diesel stocks to address short-term shortages caused by recent pipeline delivery delays, the Swiss Federal Office for National Economic Supply (FONES) said late Oct. 23.
The delays in pipeline deliveries were due to heavy rainfall in southern France. Supplies had already been "severely disrupted for months" due to low Rhine water levels.
"Drawing on mandatory reserves will help offset this temporary shortfall, ensuring that Switzerland's diesel supply remains secure," FONES said in the statement. Up to 45,000 cubic meters can be drawn between Oct. 23 and Nov. 22, FONES said. The volume corresponds to just under 4% of the available obligatory diesel stocks.
Poor weather heading into the European winter could see further supply disruptions in an already constrained market with little leeway.

EUROPE AND AFRICA RESIDUAL AND MARINE FUELS: Key market indicators for Oct. 26-30
HSFO market firms on supply constraints
Bitumen tightens on maintenance, crude slate shifts
Swaps Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month/
second-month FOB Rotterdam high sulfur fuel oil barge time spread at a backwardation of $28.25/metric ton on Oct. 26, widening $6/mt from the previous week.

HSFO European HSFO markets continued to firm as participants
noted resupply concerns stemming from a closed arbitrage window from the Americas region.
HSFO supply volumes from the East Mediterranean region, which were significant earlier in the summer, have also declined, adding to the firmer fundamentals.
Softer bunker demand across the region, however, is balancing the supply fundamentals, contributing to a generally "balanced" market.
Fuel oil stocks in the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp refining hub fell 0.85% to 930,000 metric tons in the week to Oct. 22, Insights Global data showed, after building in the previous week.
Bitumen European bitumen markets remained tight over the past week,
with one trader describing conditions as "quite tight" and citing refinery maintenance and shifts in crude slates as the key drivers.
Traders broadly noted a seasonal slowdown as winter approaches and conditions turn less suitable for road and infrastructure work, though pockets of support persisted where weather remained favorable.
In Eastern Europe, domestic demand was reported as weak, but localized tightness was drawing product across borders, according to a second trader.
VLSFO/LSFO European VLSFO fundamentals remained dictated by volumes
moving out of the region through open arbitrage windows to Singapore and the Americas.
While VLSFO volumes continued to move on these arbitrage windows, dirty tanker freight rates remained elevated, limiting those movements and the strength of 0.5% sulfur fuel oil.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

Saudi Arabia and Houthi forces exchanged airstrikes over the weekend, which has kept risk premiums high and crude prices elevated.
Significant amounts of refinery capacity remain offline in the Middle East and Russia, reinforcing concerns over product availability despite the postponement of turnarounds in Europe.
Equally, players pointed to a thin market with relatively wide bid-ask spreads, which has increased the volatility in the naphtha market.
"When the market is that thin and there's not really much, every cargo [traded] impacts the price," the trading source added.
European and Mediterranean naphtha has meanwhile been drawn toward Asia amid high east-west spreads, helping clear some surplus availability and further tightening the European balance.
Weaker gas-nap offers relief While softer propane-naphtha spreads have improved
naphtha's competitiveness as a cracker feedstock, petrochemical players continue to operate on a hand-to-mouth basis, only dipping into the buy-side when necessary.
"In Europe, the chems are a bit hand-to-mouth, in Asia as well. I think everyone is running a bit hand-to-mouth, and when they need to buy, they pay whatever price is there because they need to keep running," the trader added.
A softer gasoline complex might help quench some of the demand for naphtha in Europe, with gasoline-naphtha spreads narrowing significantly.
Naphtha blending economics have been eroded substantially as a result, reducing blender's incentive to maximize naphtha's share in the blending pool. This could potentially release more barrels into the petrochemical cracking pool.
However, deteriorating gasoline margins also pose a broader risk to refinery economics. Should weaker product margins ultimately lead refiners to cut runs, any increase in naphtha availability from reduced blending demand could be offset by lower refinery production.
"Gasoline going down also helps the cracking pool and pressures refinery margins, and this may impact the market further," the trader said. "If runs fall, it will be a disaster."
-- Barbara Fernandez-pita

India's HMEL seeks up to 55,000 mt naphtha for Nov. 8-25 delivery
Lotte Chemical to reduce Yeosu steam cracker run rate to 70% for Nov
The Asian naphtha market held firm on the back of heightened geopolitical tensions Oct. 26, while high crude prices spurred the naphtha flat price higher.

In cracker news, South Korea's Lotte Chemical will reduce its naphtha-fed steam cracker's operating rate in Yeosu to about 70% capacity in November, marginally lower than the existing run rate of about 70%-75%, Platts reported earlier. The cracker is able to produce 1.2 million metric tons/year of ethylene, 600,000 mt/ year of propylene and 160,000 mt/year of butadiene.

Platts Western Mediterranean LPG Daily Commentary
Butane supply tightens on refinery retention
FOB West Med butane rises $11/mt to $1,257
Import options remain limited amid competition
West Mediterranean butane availability remained constrained on Oct. 26 as refinery retention for winter gasoline blending limited supplies reaching the merchant market, while restricted import options offered little relief.
A trader highlighted a "seasonal increase in demand," as winter gasoline specifications continued to underpin butane requirements within refinery blending pools.
Refiners prioritized internal requirements, reducing volumes available for spot sales and making prompt cargoes difficult to source.
The trader also pointed to "difficulty sourcing available volumes."
Import availability remained constrained. Reduced Middle Eastern exports tightened the wider butane balance, while competition from Asian buyers for US LPG limited opportunities for replenishment from the Atlantic Basin.
Higher freight costs and limited availability of smaller vessels added to the difficulty of securing workable deliveries into the Mediterranean.
Petrochemical demand provided little additional support, with butane remaining unattractive relative to alternative feedstocks. As a result, the market remained primarily driven by seasonal gasoline blending requirements and restricted merchant supply.
The combination of refinery retention, limited import relief and logistical constraints maintained a firm underlying balance, while weaker petrochemical interest and selective spot buying tempered broader demand.
Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB butane market at $1,257/metric ton, up $11/mt day over day and stable as a percentage versus naphtha at 128%, or at a $39/mt discount to FOB Northwest Europe coasters.
Platts assessed coaster freight from Lavera to Mohammedia at $66/mt, steady day over day, based on market information received throughout the day.
The CIF Mediterranean butane large cargo market was

assessed at $1,129.75/mt, or 115% of naphtha. Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB propane market
at $972.50/mt, up $18/mt day over day. The CIF Med propane large cargo market was assessed at

Tankers
Saudi shipper Bahri expands tanker fleet with $451M VLCC order
Three 320,000 DWT vessels to be delivered by 2029-30
Fleet reaches 107 vessels end of June
Deal brings Bahri's newbuild orders to nine vessels
The National Shipping Co. of Saudi Arabia has signed a $451 million deal with domestic shipbuilder International Maritime Industries Co. to construct and deliver three very large crude carriers, Bahri said in a statement Oct. 25 to the Saudi stock exchange.
The order, signed on Oct. 22, will involve the manufacture of three VLCCs with a capacity of 320,000 deadweight tons each, with delivery to Bahri scheduled in batches between 2029 and 2030, it said in the statement.
Bahri's fleet, which was fully commercially deployed over the second quarter despite the Middle East conflict, stood at "a record" 107 vessels at the end of June 2026, including 49 oil carriers as of the end of the second quarter, the company said July 29 in a quarterly earnings release.
The three vessels will bring the total number of newbuild ships being constructed in Saudi Arabia for Bahri to nine, comprising six dry bulk carriers and three VLCCs, Bahri said.
In October 2025, Bahri signed a deal to purchase six geared Ultramax dry bulk vessels from IMI to be constructed in the shipbuilder's Ras al-Khair shipyard in Saudi Arabia and see delivery between 2028 and 2029, Platts reported Oct. 2, 2025.
Bahri will fund its latest contract through a combination of internal cash resources and banking facilities, according to the statement, while the contract term will end upon the delivery of the last vessel to the company.
The financial impact of the contract will be reflected on Bahri's financial statements after the vessels are received and commercial operations begin, the company said.
Bahri holds a 19.9% equity stake in IMI, a subsidiary of Saudi Arabian Oil Co., while Bahri CEO Ali al-Subaey also serves on IMI's board of directors, according to the company's statement.
-- Lulwa Albuainain

VLCC transits climb at Hormuz; steady at Bab al-Mandab for second day
Hormuz sees six VLCCs, most since Oct 17
Bab al-Mandab records one VLCC for two days
US marks 1.5 billion barrels through Hormuz
The Strait of Hormuz had six VLCC transits on Oct. 25, the most since Oct. 17, while the Bab al-Mandab Strait recorded one VLCC on both Oct. 24 and Oct. 25, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 26 report.
Hormuz traffic in total was steady at 17 ships on Oct. 24 and Oct. 25, while Bab al-Mandab transits jumped to 42 ships from 27 ships over the same period, according to the CAS report. The VLCC total for Hormuz on Oct. 25 was up from five on Oct. 24, CAS data showed.
The outbound VLCC from the Persian Gulf was Monaco Prosperity, while inbound VLCCs were Bangladesh Prosperity, Ennie, Thailand Prosperity, West Loyalty and Bulgaria Prosperity, CAS said. The VLCCs that transited Bab al-Mandab were Della, heading northbound, on Oct. 25 and Karina, also moving northbound, on Oct. 24, according to CAS.
US forces on Oct. 25 marked the export of at least 1.5 billion barrels of crude oil since the summer through Hormuz, with US Central Command commander Admiral Brad Cooper flying over the chokepoint in a US Army CH-47 Chinook, Central Command said on X. It noted that more than 3,000 commercial ships had successfully transited Hormuz with support from CENTCOM forces over the last six months.
"Today, primary transit routes in the Strait of Hormuz remain free and clear of Iranian sea mines thanks to incredible efforts by our people," Cooper said. "We remain intensely focused on keeping commercial traffic flowing as we work with our teammates in the US interagency, Gulf Cooperation Council, and shipping industry."
The comment came despite Iran launching its heaviest wave of attacks on merchant ships around Hormuz and in the wider region this month, with the International Maritime Organization recording 29 attacks during Oct. 1-22, exceeding all monthly tallies since March.
On Oct. 26, the UK Maritime Trade Operations Centre reported a tanker exiting Hormuz was disabled after being struck by an unknown projectile Oct. 23. No crew casualties or oil spills have been reported.
Of the 16 VLCC crossings identified over Oct. 23-25, 10 were assessed as inbound and six outbound, CAS said. Inbound VLCC movements included five of South Korea's Sinokor-operated ships, and the Saudi-linked Majra and Qasba. The VLCC Ennie moved into the Persian Gulf after last loading a cargo of Basrah Heavy crude in early October, and China-owned VLCC Indigo Ray also moved inbound, CAS said.
Outbound transits included Sinokor-operated Romania Prosperity, UAE-linked Das and Al Faya, and Saudi-linked Kahla and Emerald Shine, CAS said.

LPG tankers Over Oct. 23-25, five LPG tankers were observed crossing
the Strait of Hormuz, including one outbound and four inbound transits, CAS said.
The outbound ship was the Marshall Islands-flagged Very Large Gas Carrier Sheng Hai Long, which had been in the Persian Gulf since Aug. 21, CAS said. During its stay, the ship loaded LPG at Ras Laffan, Qatar, while operating without AIS visibility, then exited the strait in a dark transit before reappearing in the Arabian Sea on Oct. 24, CAS said.
On Oct. 24, two LPG tankers entered the Gulf. One was UAElinked Sweihan, which previously transferred cargo at Vadinar, India, and was last seen heading for Ruwais, UAE, for its next loading, CAS said. On Oct. 25, UAE-linked LPG tanker Delma was last seen berthed at Ras Laffan for loading, CAS said.
Activity across the US naval blockade line came to 57 transits on Oct. 25, up from 51 on Oct. 24, according to CAS.
-- Claudia Carpenter, Max Lin
Alberta backs bill to remove crude tanker ban in Canadian Pacific
Bill scheduled for debate on Oct. 28 Sees options for crude-by-rail exports
Alberta has extended its support for a private bill in the Canadian Parliament to repeal the federal Oil Tanker Moratorium Act, which is scheduled for debate on Oct. 28, a provincial government statement said Oct. 26.
The provincial government supports private member's Bill C-264, and repealing the ban would open the door to crude-byrail exports through Prince Rupert in coastal British Columbia, giving the province another route to reach customers around the world, Premier Danielle Smith said in a statement.
"Our oil to the United States is selling at a significant discount and at a disadvantage to the entire country [Canada]," Smith said. "The only effective way to close that gap is to increase exports off our western coast and get more customers competing for our energy. For decades, limited access to tidewater has left Alberta heavily dependent on a single customer, putting our oil at a competitive disadvantage and reducing the value of our resources."

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

30

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.



Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

In March, Conservative Member of Parliament David McKenzie from the Calgary Signal Hill riding in Alberta first tabled the bill for discussion in parliament.
The Conservative Party of Canada is the official opposition in parliament (House of Commons) with 137 members, with a total of 342 seats.
"The tanker ban remains a stumbling block to accessing Asia, one of the fastest-growing markets in the world," Alberta's Energy and Minerals Minister Brian Jean said in a statement. "Sailing from Prince Rupert would provide the shortest energy shipping route to Asia. This means lower shipping costs, less fuel used and fewer emissions created. Producers have already expressed interest in using this route for crude oil exports."
In June 2019, the federal government enacted the Oil Moratorium Act, which banned the movement of tankers laden with over 12,500 metric tons (91,625 barrels) of crude oil in the area from the Canada/US border in the north, down to the point on British Columbia's mainland across from the northern tip of Vancouver Island. It also included Haida Gwaii and the waters of Dixon Entrance, Hecate Strait and Queen Charlotte Sound.
The oil tanker ban does not apply to natural gas or NGL or any other commodity carried by tankers, Jean said, adding that Canada has one of the best marine safety and environmental records in the world.
"We safely manage tanker navigation in busy and difficult waters on both the west and east coasts without incident. We can also do so with oil tankers carrying Alberta oil," Jean said.
-- Ashok Dutta
Platts East of Suez Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
Chartering STS ships delayed
Cross-harbor Singapore freight falls below $1 million
MRs garner higher earnings in Singapore vs Oman
Medium Range, or MRs, were mostly lower Oct. 26 amid ample tanker supply.
South Korea remains the primary source of activity in Northeast Asia, with China's export volumes easing due to restrictions, an MR broker said.
Charterers are expected to take advantage of the small decline in freight by covering their requirements for loading early next month. Pertamina is seeking two MRs: one for Nov. 2 loading on the Singapore-Tuban route, with an option to discharge in Teluk Kabung, and another for Nov. 8 loading on the SingaporeBau Bau route, sources said. Petrolimex is seeking an MR for Nov. 4 loading on the Thailand-Ho Chi Minh route, sources added.
Returns are higher for loading cargoes in Southeast Asia for delivery Down Under than for Oman or West Coast India loading for East and South Africa delivery, said a source at a clean-oil tanker owner. Daily scrubber-fitted MR earnings on the benchmark Singapore-Australia route are close to $67,000$77,000 on a round-voyage basis, assuming HSFO bunker prices of around $875/metric ton, according to broker estimates. Corresponding daily earnings on the West Coast India/Oman-to-

East Africa route are around $57,000-$58,000, assuming HSFO bunker prices of around $730/mt, they said. For older MRs, these earnings are even lower, at around $54,000, they added.
Though Typhoon Champi is projected to reach coastlines soon, the logistical impact on shipments is expected to remain limited due to its weak intensity, a second MR broker said. The potential impact of the approaching Typhoon No. 31 on upcoming activities remains uncertain, a third broker said.
Among outstanding cargoes, Ampol is seeking an MR for Nov. 4-6 loading on the Korea-Australia route, sources said.
Southeast Asia MRs inched lower on softening sentiment for longer voyages. With slowing chartering activity and MRs ballasting toward Southeast Asia, freight is poised to soften, a clean tankers' broker said.
The Silver Monika was placed on subjects by Pertamina for Oct. 28 loading on the Singapore-Tanjung Uban route, and rate details were not known, sources said. Cross-harbor MR voyages in Singapore are expected to be below the psychological mark of $1 million, brokers said.
Long Range, or LRs, were unchanged due to slow market activity, market participants said. Due to the Hormuz War, charterers are uncertain about the fate of the "shuttle tankers" bringing out cargoes through the risky waters of the Persian Gulf, one of the brokers said. The tankers may get stuck in the narrow waterway, or their transit can get inordinately delayed due to the war, which in turn will hurt the itinerary of the shipto-ship, or STS, transfer in Oman or West Coast India, the broker said. Therefore, charterers are now hiring LRs for STS transfers outside the Persian Gulf at much shorter, or prompt, notice after they are convinced about the transit of the shuttle tankers, he said. According to market participants, this can make the overall supply of tankers look bigger than it actually is.
An LR broker said that next-done transactions on LR1s could be tracking close to recent Africa-bound voyages, citing the recent Gem No. 3 taken by Trafigura at w500.
Among outstanding cargoes, Keumeur is seeking a tanker for Oct. 27 naphtha loading on the Fujairah-East route, though it was heard downsizing to an MR instead, sources said. Shell is seeking an LR1 for end-October loading on the Sohar-West route via the Suez Canal, sources added.
In recent fixtures, the LR1 Torm Eva was placed on subjects at w535 for Oct. 28 loading on the STS Oman-Japan route, and the Amazon Fortitude was replaced by the Hafnia Excellence at w550 by OQ after being released, sources said.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Sameer Mohindru, Ellie Lin

"V's in Brazil definitely a bit firmer into end November windows, primarily due to increased rates and demand in [the Gulf of Oman]," the second broker added.
Platts assessed the 260,000 mt Brazil-China run up w5 to w535.
Market indications for the VLCC USGC-China route were heard between $55 million-$57.5 million, with a number of brokers holding their positions steady from Oct. 23 amid a lack of fresh testing since Brazil rates rose.
Platts assessed the benchmark 270,000 mt USGC-China run steady at $57.5 million.
Aframaxes lack testing Aframax rates were mostly steady on the day as firm
sentiment supported rates amid limited reported fixing activity. At least four transatlantic cargoes, all loading in the first half of November and three from the US Gulf, were reported working. Platts did not report any Americas Aframax or Panamax fixtures for the second straight session.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

In Q3 the margin was $34.00/b as middle distillate cracks surged, and Galp said its system operated at close to full rates to capture "elevated" international products cracks, particularly for diesel and jet.
The company said in July it expected throughput for the full year in a range between 80 million barrels and 85 million barrels (about 219,000-233,000 b/d) following robust operations in Q2, with no update provided since.
The 226,000 b/d Sines refinery started a hydrocracker maintenance Sept. 24 that is expected to end around Nov. 1, but "no major turnaround [is] planned to 2027," co-CEO Joao Diogo Marques da Silva told analysts on a conference call.
Galp said it has "been actively mitigating global market disruptions, having already secured all Portugal's jet import requirements for the remainder of the year."
The margin currently sits at around $20/b, Marques da Silva said, noting that there are "sustainable fundamentals to assume double-digit margin to 2027, at least in the first quarter."
The company is also in the "final ramp up of the major investments" that it is making in Sines, Marques da Silva said.
Galp plans to bring online two low-carbon projects at the industrial complex, a 270,000 metric ton/year advanced biofuels unit for HVO/SAF production and a 100 MW electrolyzer plant for the production of green hydrogen.
"We are in the pre-commissioning phase right now," Marques da Silva said, without adding details.
Upstream update In the upstream business, Galp's "execution remains firmly
on track," co-CEO Maria Joao Carioca said. Galp has forecast reaching around 130,000 b/d of oil equivalent from its Brazilian upstream operations and hit an average 129,000 boe/d working interest production in the first nine months of 2026.
Ramp-up of the Equinor-operated Bacalhau project in the Santos basin is "progressing as planned, with production already reaching peak levels on several days [..] with four producer wells connected," Carioca said.
The field has a production target of 220,000 boe/d by yearend, with 40,000 boe/d net to Galp. Equinor did not respond to a request for comment from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
Galp has added to its Brazil portfolio through the award of the Rodocrosita block, neighboring Bacalhau, which should secure additional long-term optionality and the potential to unlock future synergies.
Galp declined to give upstream guidance for 2027, but noted that Bacalhau should produce at plateau, while other Brazilian assets are seeing depletion rates of less than 5%.
In Namibia, preparation works for the next exploration and appraisal campaign at Mopane continue with the drilling of the next well imminent, Galp said, while a final investment decision for the Venus field could be taken "soon," according to Carioca.
The company's midstream operations had seen "disrupted trading dynamics, in particular elevated freight costs and softer crude premiums amid weaker Asian demand," the company noted.

Its oil realization indicator, estimated by applying the average discount or premium to Brent of all negotiated cargoes, net of logistics costs, to the average Brent price for the same period, was $87.30/b in Q3, narrowing from $102.90/b in Q2.
Freight costs have risen from around $3-$4/b to over $20/b in October, which has led the company to consider "options" in terms of extending mid-term charters, and not rely so much on the spot market, Carioca said.
-- Gianluca Baratti
INDIA DATA: Indian refineries processed 5.62 mil b/d in Sep: Oil Min
Crude processed in Sep up 9.1% on year, up 2.7% on month
Sep crude imports 10.2% up on year, up 20% on month
Sep oil demand 2% up on year, 6.4% up on month
Indian refineries processed 5.62 million b/d in September, the oil ministry's provisional estimates showed, reflecting 9.1% higher processing from a year ago as imports by the world's third-largest buyer rose despite supply concerns due to the Middle East conflict.
The crude processed in September was 6.6% higher than the month's target of 5.27 million b/d, it said.
The processed volume in September was 2.7% higher than 5.47 million b/d in August, the ministry said.
In the April-September period or H1, Indian refiners processed 5.45 million b/d, up 1.5% from a year ago, it said, reflecting improved runs during the first half of the fiscal year despite maintenance works carried out by private refiners like Nayara and Reliance during the first quarter.
India processed 1.3% year-over-year higher crude volume at 5.46 million b/d in 2025-26 (April-March) or FY26, according to the ministry's provisional data, aided by cheaper Russian crude imports.
India, the world's fourth-largest refiner, has 5.35 million b/d installed crude processing capacity, according to the oil ministry.
India, also the world's third-largest crude consumer and importer, sources 88% of its domestic crude demand through imports, it said.
Imports, domestic demand In September, Indian refiners imported 5.4 million b/d,
according to the oil ministry's data, up 10.2% year over year and up 20% from August.
In the April-September period or H1, Indian refiners imported 4.9 million b/d, the same volume as a year ago, it said.
Indian refineries imported 4.9 million b/d in FY26, the same as a year ago, the latest provisional data showed.
In September, India's oil product demand stood at 5 million b/d, up 2% year over year, the oil ministry said.
In H1, India's oil demand stood at 4.9 million b/d, down 3.9% from a year ago, it said.
India's oil product demand was 5.2 million b/d in FY26, up 2% from a year ago, the ministry said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference

between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

Assessment Gasoline STS Lome Diff (EBOB) Gasoline STS Lome Diff (EBOB) MAvg

Description Gasoline STS Lome vs Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge Gasoline STS Lome vs Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge MAvg

Symbol To be announced
To be announced

Platts will assess the differential in $/metric ton, reflecting the difference between the relevant STS Lome gasoline assessment and the physical Platts Eurobob FOB AR assessment.
Platts will publish the new assessments in $/mt, timestamped at 4.30 pm London time, as well as a monthly average.
The new gasoline assessments will be found in European Marketscan and on Platts Global Alert and Platts Refined Products Alert fixed page 1122.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
